  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sita adalah dewi Hindu dan tokoh perempuan utama dalam Ramayana. Siya, Janaki, dan Maithili merupakan nama lain Sita.",
      "en": "Sita is a Hindu goddess and the principal female character of the Ramayana. Siya, Janaki, and Maithili are other names of Sita.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sita-c01",
        "sita-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia adalah pasangan Rama dan dipandang sebagai penjelmaan Lakshmi. Sita disebut putri Bhumi dan anak angkat Raja Janaka dari Videha. Ia memilih Rama dari Ayodhya sebagai suami dalam svayamvara. Ia mengikuti pengasingan Rama bersama Lakshmana. Ravana menculiknya dari hutan Dandaka dan menahannya di taman Ashoka Vatika di Lanka. Dalam sebagian versi, Sita membuktikan kesuciannya melalui ujian api Agni Pariksha. Versi lain menyebut Maya Sita menggantikannya saat diculik, sementara Sita asli berlindung dalam api. Sejumlah teks menghubungkan kehidupan terdahulunya dengan Vedavati. Dalam kelanjutan kisah, Sita berlindung di asrama Valmiki dan melahirkan anak kembar Kusha dan Lava. Akhir kisah tersebut menuturkan kembalinya Sita ke bumi, ibunya. Nama Sita berasal dari kata Sanskerta yang berarti alur bajakan. Mackenzie juga menempatkan Sita sebagai penjelmaan manusia Lakshmi dalam Ramayana. Lukisan yang dibahas Museum Carlos menggambarkan Ravana membawa Sita dari pertapaan di hutan.",
      "en": "She is Rama's consort and is regarded as an incarnation of Lakshmi. Sita is described as Bhumi's daughter and King Janaka of Videha's adopted child. She chooses Rama of Ayodhya as her husband in a svayamvara. She accompanies Rama into exile with Lakshmana. Ravana abducts her from the Dandaka forest and holds her in Ashoka Vatika garden in Lanka. In some versions, Sita demonstrates her chastity through the Agni Pariksha fire ordeal. Other versions say Maya Sita replaces her during the abduction while the real Sita shelters in fire. Some texts connect her previous birth with Vedavati. In the continuation, Sita shelters in Valmiki's ashram and gives birth to twins Kusha and Lava. That ending recounts Sita's return to the earth, her mother. The name Sita derives from a Sanskrit word meaning furrow. Mackenzie also places Sita as Lakshmi's human incarnation in the Ramayana. The painting discussed by the Carlos Museum depicts Ravana carrying Sita away from a forest hermitage.",

      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "Described as the daughter of Bhūmi (the earth), Sita is brought up as the adopted daughter of King Janaka of Videha. After the svayamvara, she accompanies her husband to his kingdom but later chooses to accompany him along with her brother-in-law Lakshmana in his exile.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia mengikuti pengasingan Rama bersama Lakshmana.",
        "en": "She accompanies Rama into exile with Lakshmana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c07",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "While in exile, the trio settles in the Dandaka forest from where she is abducted by Ravana, the Rakshasa king of Lanka. She is imprisoned in the garden of Ashoka Vatika in Lanka until she is rescued by Rama, who slays her captor.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ravana menculiknya dari hutan Dandaka dan menahannya di taman Ashoka Vatika di Lanka.",
        "en": "Ravana abducts her from the Dandaka forest and holds her in Ashoka Vatika garden in Lanka."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c08",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "After the war, in some versions of the epic, Rama asks Sita to undergo Agni Pariksha (an ordeal of fire), by which she proves her chastity, before she is accepted by Rama. Such an episode of trial makes Lakshmana angry at Rama, for the first time.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sebagian versi, Sita membuktikan kesuciannya melalui ujian api Agni Pariksha.",
        "en": "In some versions, Sita demonstrates her chastity through the Agni Pariksha fire ordeal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c09",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "In some other versions of the epic, Maya Sita, an illusion created by Agni, takes Sita's place and is abducted by Ravana and suffers his captivity, while the real Sita hides in the fire.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi lain menyebut Maya Sita menggantikannya saat diculik, sementara Sita asli berlindung dalam api.",
        "en": "Other versions say Maya Sita replaces her during the abduction while the real Sita shelters in fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c10",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "Some scriptures also mention her previous birth as Vedavati, a woman Ravana tries to molest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah teks menghubungkan kehidupan terdahulunya dengan Vedavati.",
        "en": "Some texts connect her previous birth with Vedavati."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c11",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "She takes shelter at Sage Valmiki's ashram. She gives birth to twin sons there: Kusha and Lava.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kelanjutan kisah, Sita berlindung di asrama Valmiki dan melahirkan anak kembar Kusha dan Lava.",
        "en": "In the continuation, Sita shelters in Valmiki's ashram and gives birth to twins Kusha and Lava."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c12",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "Years later, Sita reunites her two sons with their father Rama and then returns to the womb of her mother, the earth, as a testimony to her ultimate purity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Akhir kisah tersebut menuturkan kembalinya Sita ke bumi, ibunya.",
        "en": "That ending recounts Sita's return to the earth, her mother."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c13",
      "source_id": "sita-s1",
      "quote": "The goddess is best known by the name \"Sita\", derived from the Sanskrit word sīta, (\"furrow\".)",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Sita berasal dari kata Sanskerta yang berarti alur bajakan.",
        "en": "The name Sita derives from a Sanskrit word meaning furrow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c14",
      "source_id": "sita-s2",
      "quote": "She has had several\r\nhuman incarnations, and in each case was loved by the\r\nincarnation of Vishnu. She is Sita in the Ramáyana, and\r\nthe beautiful herdswoman beloved by Krishna.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 821",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie juga menempatkan Sita sebagai penjelmaan manusia Lakshmi dalam Ramayana.",
        "en": "Mackenzie also places Sita as Lakshmi's human incarnation in the Ramayana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sita-c15",
      "source_id": "sita-s3",
      "quote": "Ravana carries Sita away from her opulent hermitage in the highly stylized forest",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",

      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Varuna adalah dewa Hindu yang digambarkan sebagai penguasa langit pada zaman Weda awal.",
    "en": "Varuna is a Hindu god depicted as sovereign of the sky in early Vedic times.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "varuna-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Varuna adalah dewa Hindu yang digambarkan sebagai penguasa langit pada zaman Weda awal. Ia menjaga kebenaran dan rta, tatanan kosmis serta moral.",
      "en": "Varuna is a Hindu god depicted as sovereign of the sky in early Vedic times. He maintains truth and rta, cosmic and moral order.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "varuna-c01",
        "varuna-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Varuna sering dipasangkan dengan Mitra. Dalam tradisi Itihasa dan Purana ia menjadi penguasa lautan, sungai, aliran air, dan danau. Ia juga berperan sebagai dikpala penjaga arah barat. Penggambarannya mencakup sosok muda yang menunggang makhluk Makara. Ia membawa jerat pasha dan kendi. Tolkappiyam menyebutnya Kadalon, dewa laut dan hujan. Dalam Buddhisme Jepang ia dikenal sebagai Suiten dan termasuk Dua Belas Dewa. Penjelasan Hindu mengaitkan namanya dengan akar vr yang berarti meliputi atau mengikat. Rigveda memuat sejumlah himne Varuna dan menggambarkannya sebagai raja langit, penjaga rta, penguasa air, serta pemilik maya. Rigveda 1.25 memujinya karena memahami lintasan burung, kapal, dan angin. Himne menggambarkannya menghukum pelanggaran tetapi mengasihi orang yang bertobat. Britannica 1911 mencatat gelar Jalapati dan Amburaja yang merujuk penguasa air. Patung Varuna di NGV berupa sosok batu pasir merah dalam lengkung berpilar, diapit penjaga dan makhluk leogryph.",
      "en": "Varuna is frequently paired with Mitra. In Itihasa and Purana traditions he becomes lord of oceans, rivers, streams, and lakes. He also serves as the dikpala guarding the west. His depictions include a youthful figure riding the creature Makara. He carries a pasha noose and pitcher. The Tolkappiyam calls him Kadalon, god of sea and rain. In Japanese Buddhism he is known as Suiten and belongs to the Twelve Devas. A Hindu explanation connects his name with the root vr, meaning to encompass or bind. The Rigveda contains several Varuna hymns and depicts him as sky sovereign, guardian of rta, water deity, and wielder of maya. Rigveda 1.25 praises him for understanding the paths of birds, ships, and winds. Hymns depict him as punishing wrongdoing while showing mercy to the penitent. The 1911 Britannica records the epithets Jalapati and Amburaja referring to lordship over water. The NGV Varuna sculpture is a red sandstone figure in a columned arch, flanked by guardians and leogryphs.",

      "publisher": "Cambridge University Press, Encyclopaedia Britannica 1911; Wikisource",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "encyclopedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-s3",
      "url": "https://www.ngv.vic.gov.au/explore/collection/work/52794/",
      "title": "Varuna, INDIAN | NGV",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "National Gallery of Victoria",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "museum-or-archive",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "varuna-c01",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "In the early Vedic era, Varuna is seen as the god-sovereign, ruling the sky and embodying divine authority.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Varuna adalah dewa Hindu yang digambarkan sebagai penguasa langit pada zaman Weda awal.",
        "en": "Varuna is a Hindu god depicted as sovereign of the sky in early Vedic times."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c02",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "He maintains truth and ṛta, the cosmic and moral order, and was invoked as an omniscient ethical judge, with the stars symbolizing his watchful eyes or spies.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjaga kebenaran dan rta, tatanan kosmis serta moral.",
        "en": "He maintains truth and rta, cosmic and moral order."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c03",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "Frequently paired with Mitra, Varuna represents the magical and speculative aspects of sovereignty, overseeing the relationship between gods and humans.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Varuna sering dipasangkan dengan Mitra.",
        "en": "Varuna is frequently paired with Mitra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c04",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "By the time of the Itihasa-Purana, Varuna had transformed into the lord of all waters, ruling over oceans, rivers, streams, and lakes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Itihasa dan Purana ia menjadi penguasa lautan, sungai, aliran air, dan danau.",
        "en": "In Itihasa and Purana traditions he becomes lord of oceans, rivers, streams, and lakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c05",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "Varuna’s earlier supremacy diminished, and he was relegated to a lesser role as a dikpala, or guardian of the western direction.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga berperan sebagai dikpala penjaga arah barat.",
        "en": "He also serves as the dikpala guarding the west."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c06",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "He is depicted as a youthful man, mounted on Makara (crocodile-like creature) and holding a Pasha (noose, rope loop) and a pitcher in his hands.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya mencakup sosok muda yang menunggang makhluk Makara.",
        "en": "His depictions include a youthful figure riding the creature Makara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c07",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "He is depicted as a youthful man, mounted on Makara (crocodile-like creature) and holding a Pasha (noose, rope loop) and a pitcher in his hands.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa jerat pasha dan kendi.",
        "en": "He carries a pasha noose and pitcher."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c08",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "Varuna is also mentioned in the Tamil grammar work Tolkāppiyam, as Kadalon (Tamil: கடலோன், romanized: Kaṭalōṉ), the god of sea and rain, and is furthermore present as a deity in Jainism. In Japanese Buddhist myth, Varuna is known as Suiten (水天; lit. \"Water Deva\") and ranks among the Twelve Devas (Jūniten).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tolkappiyam menyebutnya Kadalon, dewa laut dan hujan.",
        "en": "The Tolkappiyam calls him Kadalon, god of sea and rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c09",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "Varuna is also mentioned in the Tamil grammar work Tolkāppiyam, as Kadalon (Tamil: கடலோன், romanized: Kaṭalōṉ), the god of sea and rain, and is furthermore present as a deity in Jainism. In Japanese Buddhist myth, Varuna is known as Suiten (水天; lit. \"Water Deva\") and ranks among the Twelve Devas (Jūniten).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Buddhisme Jepang ia dikenal sebagai Suiten dan termasuk Dua Belas Dewa.",
        "en": "In Japanese Buddhism he is known as Suiten and belongs to the Twelve Devas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c10",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu tradition, the theonym Váruṇa (Devanagari: वरुण) is described as a derivation from the verbal root vṛ (\"to surround, to cover\" or \"to restrain, bind\") by means of a suffixal -uṇa-, for an interpretation of the name as  \"he who covers or binds\", in reference to the cosmological ocean or river encircling the world, but also in reference to the \"binding\" by universal law or Ṛta.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penjelasan Hindu mengaitkan namanya dengan akar vr yang berarti meliputi atau mengikat.",
        "en": "A Hindu explanation connects his name with the root vr, meaning to encompass or bind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c11",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "Varuṇa is portrayed in four principal aspects: as a universal monarch and sovereign of the sky, the upholder of ṛta (cosmic order), a deity associated with water (āpah), and a wielder of māyā (cosmic illusion or creative power).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda memuat sejumlah himne Varuna dan menggambarkannya sebagai raja langit, penjaga rta, penguasa air, serta pemilik maya.",
        "en": "The Rigveda contains several Varuna hymns and depicts him as sky sovereign, guardian of rta, water deity, and wielder of maya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c12",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "In Rigveda 1.25, Varuṇa is praised for his understanding of the paths of birds, the movement of ships and winds, and the secrets of time and space. Hymns characterize him as a sovereign deity, intimately acquainted with both the sacred and the profane.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda 1.25 memujinya karena memahami lintasan burung, kapal, dan angin.",
        "en": "Rigveda 1.25 praises him for understanding the paths of birds, ships, and winds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c13",
      "source_id": "varuna-s1",
      "quote": "Varuṇa's foremost role is as the enforcer of ṛta, the cosmic and moral order that governs both the natural world and human conduct. Hymns such as Rigveda VII.11.1 and II.29.8 present him as a vigilant upholder of truth, who punishes transgressors while showing mercy to the penitent. Ethical instructions against killing, deceit, and gambling are linked with his domain.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Himne menggambarkannya menghukum pelanggaran tetapi mengasihi orang yang bertobat.",
        "en": "Hymns depict him as punishing wrongdoing while showing mercy to the penitent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c14",
      "source_id": "varuna-s2",
      "quote": "Among Varuna's aliases are Jalapati, \"Lord of Water,\" and \nAmburaja, \" King of Water.\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Britannica 1911 mencatat gelar Jalapati dan Amburaja yang merujuk penguasa air.",
        "en": "The 1911 Britannica records the epithets Jalapati and Amburaja referring to lordship over water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "varuna-c15",
      "source_id": "varuna-s3",
      "quote": "Physical description\n\t\tFigure of Varuna, red sandstone. Standing in columned arch, flanked by guardians and leogryphs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 13",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Patung Varuna di NGV berupa sosok batu pasir merah dalam lengkung berpilar, diapit penjaga dan makhluk leogryph.",
        "en": "The NGV Varuna sculpture is a red sandstone figure in a columned arch, flanked by guardians and leogryphs."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {

    }
  ]
}

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kartikeya adalah dewa perang Hindu yang umumnya disebut putra Shiva dan Parvati. Ia juga dikenal sebagai Skanda, Subrahmanya, Shanmukha, dan Muruga.",
      "en": "Kartikeya is a Hindu war god generally described as the son of Shiva and Parvati. He is also known as Skanda, Subrahmanya, Shanmukha, and Muruga.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kartikeya-c01",
        "kartikeya-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ganesha disebut saudaranya. Kaumaram adalah aliran Hindu yang terutama memuja Kartikeya. Artikel mengaitkan Muruga dalam sastra Sangam Tamil dengan wilayah Kurinji. Ia lazim digambarkan sebagai pemuda dengan merak bernama Paravani. Ia membawa tombak vel yang dikaitkan dengan pemberian Parvati. Sebagian gambar menunjukkan satu kepala, sebagian lainnya enam. Ia memimpin pasukan deva melawan asura termasuk Tarakasura dan Surapadma. Nama Kartikeya berarti milik para Krittika, pengasuhnya dalam cerita kelahiran. Salah satu cerita menyebut Parvati menyatukan enam bayi menjadi Kartikeya berkepala enam. Versi Vana Parva Mahabharata menyebutnya putra Agni dan Svaha. Pemujaan Kartikeya juga terdapat di Sri Lanka, Malaysia, Singapura, dan Indonesia. Museum Bihar membahas patung Kartikeya batu pasir merah dari masa Gupta akhir dengan tombak dan merak. Mackenzie merangkum versi yang menghubungkan Kartikeya dengan Agni dan Gangga.",
      "en": "Ganesha is described as his brother. Kaumaram is a Hindu denomination that primarily venerates Kartikeya. The article connects Muruga in Tamil Sangam literature with the Kurinji region. He is commonly depicted as a young man with a peacock named Paravani. He carries the vel spear associated with Parvati's gift. Some images show one head, while others show six. He leads the devas' army against asuras including Tarakasura and Surapadma. Kartikeya means of the Krittikas, his nurses in the birth narrative. One account says Parvati merges six babies into six headed Kartikeya. The Vana Parva version of the Mahabharata describes him as the son of Agni and Svaha. Kartikeya worship also occurs in Sri Lanka, Malaysia, Singapore, and Indonesia. The Bihar Museum discusses a red sandstone Kartikeya sculpture of the Later Gupta period with spear and peacock. Mackenzie summarises a version connecting Kartikeya with Agni and the Ganges.",

    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c05",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "Muruga is a tutelary deity mentioned in Tamil Sangam literature, of the Kurinji region.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",

    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c06",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "The iconography of Kartikeya varies significantly. He is typically represented as an ever-youthful man, riding or near an Peacock (named Paravani), and sometimes with an emblem of a rooster on his banner. He wields a spear called the vel, supposedly given to him by his mother Parvati.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia lazim digambarkan sebagai pemuda dengan merak bernama Paravani.",
        "en": "He is commonly depicted as a young man with a peacock named Paravani."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c07",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "The iconography of Kartikeya varies significantly. He is typically represented as an ever-youthful man, riding or near an Peacock (named Paravani), and sometimes with an emblem of a rooster on his banner. He wields a spear called the vel, supposedly given to him by his mother Parvati.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membawa tombak vel yang dikaitkan dengan pemberian Parvati.",
        "en": "He carries the vel spear associated with Parvati's gift."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c08",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "While most icons represent him with only one head, some have six heads, a reflection of legends surrounding his birth wherein he was fused from six boys or borne of six conceptions.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian gambar menunjukkan satu kepala, sebagian lainnya enam.",
        "en": "Some images show one head, while others show six."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c09",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "He is described to have aged quickly from childhood, becoming a warrior, leading the army of the devas and credited with destroying asuras including Tarakasura and Surapadma.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia memimpin pasukan deva melawan asura termasuk Tarakasura dan Surapadma.",
        "en": "He leads the devas' army against asuras including Tarakasura and Surapadma."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c10",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "Kartikeya means \"of the Krittikas\" in Sanskrit.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Kartikeya berarti milik para Krittika, pengasuhnya dalam cerita kelahiran.",
        "en": "Kartikeya means of the Krittikas, his nurses in the birth narrative."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c11",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "The epithet \"Kartikeya\" is linked to the circumstances surrounding the deity's birth. According to the Skanda Purana, six divine sparks emerged from Shiva, forming six separate baby boys. These boys were raised by handmaidens known as the Krittikas. Later, Parvati fused them into one, creating the six-headed Kartikeya. Kartikeya means \"of the Krittikas\" in Sanskrit.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu cerita menyebut Parvati menyatukan enam bayi menjadi Kartikeya berkepala enam.",
        "en": "One account says Parvati merges six babies into six headed Kartikeya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c12",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "An alternate account of Kartikeya's parentage is narrated in the Vana Parva of the Mahabharata, where he is described as the son of Agni and Svaha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Vana Parva Mahabharata menyebutnya putra Agni dan Svaha.",
        "en": "The Vana Parva version of the Mahabharata describes him as the son of Agni and Svaha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c13",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s1",
      "quote": "He is also worshipped in Sri Lanka, Southeast Asia (notably in Malaysia, Singapore, Thailand and Indonesia), other countries with significant populations of Tamil origin (including Fiji, Mauritius, South Africa and Canada), Caribbean countries (including Trinidad and Tobago, Guyana and Suriname), and countries with significant Indian migrant populations (including the United States and A",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan Kartikeya juga terdapat di Sri Lanka, Malaysia, Singapura, dan Indonesia.",
        "en": "Kartikeya worship also occurs in Sri Lanka, Malaysia, Singapore, and Indonesia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c14",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s2",
      "quote": "Kartikeya- This statue, from the Later Gupta period, is made of red sandstone. ... He is a red god seated on a blue peacock, and his weapon, a spear, is engraved on the left of the sculpture.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Museum Bihar membahas patung Kartikeya batu pasir merah dari masa Gupta akhir dengan tombak dan merak.",
        "en": "The Bihar Museum discusses a red sandstone Kartikeya sculpture of the Later Gupta period with spear and peacock."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kartikeya-c15",
      "source_id": "kartikeya-s3",
      "quote": "Another son of Shiva and Parvati is Kartikeya, the\r\nCelestial general and slayer of demons. He is also\r\nregarded as the son of Agni and the Ganges.",

      "id": "Ravana adalah raja rakshasa Lanka berkepala sepuluh dan lawan utama dalam Ramayana. Ia disebut putra Vishrava dan Kaikasi.",
      "en": "Ravana is the ten headed rakshasa king of Lanka and principal antagonist in the Ramayana. He is described as the son of Vishrava and Kaikasi.",

      "id": "Ia menculik Sita dan menahannya di Ashoka Vatika. Rama melawannya dengan dukungan Sugriva dan pasukan vanara lalu membunuhnya. Kekalahannya diperingati dengan pembakaran patung Ravana pada Dussehra. Ravana dipandang sebagai pemuja Shiva. Ia juga hadir dalam Lankavatara Sutra, Jataka Buddha, dan Ramayana Jain. Nama Ravana diterangkan berarti mengaum. Gelar Dashanana berarti yang berkepala sepuluh. Nama lainnya mencakup Lankapati dan Lankeshwara.       "en": "He abducts Sita and holds her in Ashoka Vatika. Rama fights him with Sugriva and the vanara army's support and kills him. The name Ravana is explained as meaning roaring. The epithet Dashanana means the one with ten heads. His other names include Lankapati and Lankeshwara. Brahma grants him immunity against his creations except humans in the story. Brahma's gifts also include weapons, a chariot, and shapeshifting.       "source_id": "ravana-s1",
      "quote": "His epic defeat is celebrated yearly by burning a large effigy, depicting the ten-headed Ravana, during the Hindu festival of Dussehra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ravana dipandang sebagai pemuja Shiva.",
        "en": "Ravana is regarded as a devotee of Shiva."

      "quote": "Ravana was well-versed in the six shastras and the four Vedas, including the Shiva Tandava Stotra. Ravana is also considered to be an ardent devotee of Shiva.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",

      "statement": {

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",

      "quote": "Dashānana was a title later taken on by Ravana, and it means \"the one with ten (dasha) Heads (anana)\".       "context": "etymology",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Yama adalah dewa kematian dan keadilan Hindu yang dikaitkan dengan hukuman di Naraka.",
    "en": "Yama is the Hindu god of death and justice associated with punishment in Naraka.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "yama-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yama adalah dewa kematian dan keadilan Hindu yang dikaitkan dengan hukuman di Naraka. Kala dan Dharmaraja merupakan nama lain Yama.",
      "en": "Yama is the Hindu god of death and justice associated with punishment in Naraka. Kala and Dharmaraja are other names of Yama.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yama-c01",
        "yama-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi Weda ia adalah manusia pertama yang meninggal dan menemukan jalan ke tempat orang mati. Ia disebut saudara kembar Yami serta putra Surya dan Sanjna. Ia menilai jiwa dan menempatkannya menurut perbuatan ke alam leluhur, Naraka, atau kelahiran kembali. Yama menjaga arah selatan sebagai salah satu Lokapala. Ia sering digambarkan berkulit gelap dan menunggang kerbau. Jerat atau gada menjadi perlengkapan untuk menangkap jiwa dalam penggambarannya. Yama berarti kembar dalam salah satu penjelasan etimologinya. Pashi adalah sebutan yang bermakna pembawa jerat. Yama kerap diidentifikasi dengan Dharmadeva dalam tradisi. Vettam Mani membedakan Yama dan Dharmadeva sebagai dua dewa dengan kisah asal berbeda. Sosok Yama diadaptasi sebagai raja neraka dalam mitologi Buddha dan sejumlah tradisi Asia Timur serta Tibet. Dalam kisah Savitri yang diceritakan Mackenzie, Yama melepaskan Satyavan dan menjanjikan hidup panjang bagi pasangan itu. Britannica 1911 menyebut tiga himne Rigveda ditujukan kepada Yama.",
      "en": "In Vedic tradition he is the first mortal to die and find the path to the abode of the dead. He is described as Yami's twin and the son of Surya and Sanjna. He judges souls and assigns them according to their deeds to the ancestors' realm, Naraka, or rebirth. Yama guards the south as one of the Lokapalas. He is often depicted with dark skin and riding a buffalo. A noose or mace is equipment for capturing souls in his depictions. Yama means twin in one explanation of his name. Pashi is an epithet meaning bearer of a noose. Yama is often identified with Dharmadeva in tradition. Vettam Mani distinguishes Yama and Dharmadeva as two deities with different origin stories. Yama's figure is adapted as king of hell in Buddhist mythology and several East Asian and Tibetan traditions. In the Savitri story retold by Mackenzie, Yama releases Satyavan and promises the couple long life. The 1911 Britannica says three Rigveda hymns are addressed to Yama.",

      "quote": "Yama (Sanskrit: यम, lit. 'twin'), also known as Kāla and Dharmarāja, is the Hindu god of death and justice, responsible for the dispensation of law and punishment of sinners in his abode, Naraka. He is often identified with Dharmadeva, the personification of Dharma, though the two deities have different origins and myths.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yama adalah dewa kematian dan keadilan Hindu yang dikaitkan dengan hukuman di Naraka.",

    {

      "quote": "Yama (Sanskrit: यम, lit. 'twin'), also known as Kāla and Dharmarāja, is the Hindu god of death and justice, responsible for the dispensation of law and punishment of sinners in his abode, Naraka. He is often identified with Dharmadeva, the personification of Dharma, though the two deities have different origins and myths.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kala dan Dharmaraja merupakan nama lain Yama.",

      "id": "yama-c03",
      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "In Vedic tradition, Yama was considered the first mortal who died and espied the way to the celestial abodes; as a result, he became the ruler of the departed. His role, characteristics, and abode have been expounded in texts such as the Upanishads, the Ramayana, the Mahabharata, and the Puranas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Weda ia adalah manusia pertama yang meninggal dan menemukan jalan ke tempat orang mati.",
        "en": "In Vedic tradition he is the first mortal to die and find the path to the abode of the dead."

      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "Yama is described as the twin of the goddess Yami, and the son of the god Surya (sun) (in earlier traditions Vivasvat) and Sanjna.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      "quote": "Yama is described as the twin of the goddess Yami, and the son of the god Surya (sun) (in earlier traditions Vivasvat) and Sanjna. He judges the souls of the dead and, depending on their deeds, assigns them to the realm of the Pitris (forefathers), Naraka (hell), or to be reborn on the earth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "yama-c06",
      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "Yama is one of the Lokapalas (guardians of the realms), appointed as the protector of the south direction. He is often depicted as a dark-complexioned man riding a buffalo and carrying a noose or mace to capture souls.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {

      "source_id": "yama-s1",

      "id": "yama-c09",
      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "The word \"Yama\" means 'twin' (Yama has a twin sister, Yami), and later came to mean 'binder' (derived from \"yam\"); the word also means 'moral rule or duty' (i.e. dharma), 'self-control', 'forbearance', and 'cessation'.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "Yama is also known by many other names, including Kala ('time'), Pashi (one who carries a noose') and Dharmaraja ('lord of Dharma').",

      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "Yama and Dharmadeva, the god personifying the concept of Dharma, are generally considered to be one and the same person. Author Vettam Mani speculates a reason for this identification:",

    {

      "source_id": "yama-s1",

      "source_id": "yama-s1",
      "quote": "Yama was subsequently adopted by Buddhist, Chinese, Tibetan, Korean, and Japanese mythology as the king of hell. In modern culture, Yama has been depicted in various safety campaigns in India.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Radha adalah dewi Hindu dan pasangan utama Krishna. Radhika merupakan nama lain Radha.",
      "en": "Radha is a Hindu goddess and Krishna's chief consort. Radhika is another name for Radha.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "radha-c01",
        "radha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia dipuja sebagai dewi cinta, kelembutan, kasih sayang, dan bakti. Sejumlah kitab menyebutnya penjelmaan Lakshmi dan daya batin Krishna yang disebut hladini shakti. Nimbarka dan banyak tradisi Waisnawa memujanya sebagai istri abadi Krishna. Tradisi Gaudiya Waisnawa menekankan Radha sebagai kekasih serta pasangan ilahi Krishna. Radha Vallabha dan Haridasi memujanya sebagai sosok tertinggi. Ia disebut pemimpin para gopi Braj dan ratu Goloka serta Braj. Nama Radha dijelaskan bermakna kemakmuran, keberhasilan, kesempurnaan, dan kekayaan. Narada Pancharatra mencatat 1008 namanya dalam Shri Radha Saharsnama Strotam. Pada gerakan Bhakti abad keenam belas, cinta Radha kepada Krishna menjadi lebih menonjol. Gita Govinda karya Jayadeva pada abad kedua belas menjadi salah satu penampilan sastra utamanya. Kajian yang diringkas artikel membedakan Radha sebagai gopi dan sebagai dewi. Kajian Archer menyebut Gita Govinda menuturkan perpisahan, kerinduan, dan pertemuan kembali Radha dengan Krishna. Terjemahan Arnold menggambarkan Radha menanti Krishna di tepi hutan pada musim semi.",
      "en": "She is revered as a goddess of love, tenderness, compassion, and devotion. Some scriptures call her Lakshmi's incarnation and Krishna's internal potency called hladini shakti. Nimbarka and many Vaishnava traditions worship her as Krishna's eternal wife. Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition emphasises Radha as Krishna's beloved and divine consort. Radha Vallabha and Haridasi venerate her as the supreme being. She is called chief of the Braj gopis and queen of Goloka and Braj. Radha's name is explained as prosperity, success, perfection, and wealth. The Narada Pancharatra records 1008 of her names in the Shri Radha Saharsnama Strotam. In the sixteenth century Bhakti movement, Radha's love for Krishna became more prominent. Jayadeva's twelfth century Gita Govinda is one of her major literary appearances. Scholarship summarised by the article distinguishes Radha as a gopi and as a goddess. Archer's study says the Gita Govinda recounts separation, longing, and reunion between Radha and Krishna. Arnold's translation depicts Radha awaiting Krishna by the wood in spring.",

        "en": "She is revered as a goddess of love, tenderness, compassion, and devotion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c04",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "In scriptures, Radha is mentioned as the avatar of Lakshmi and also as the Mūlaprakriti, the Supreme goddess, who is the feminine counterpart and internal potency (hladini shakti) of Krishna.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejumlah kitab menyebutnya penjelmaan Lakshmi dan daya batin Krishna yang disebut hladini shakti.",
        "en": "Some scriptures call her Lakshmi's incarnation and Krishna's internal potency called hladini shakti."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c05",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "Radha accompanies Krishna in all his incarnations. The Nimbarka Sampradaya and many other Vaishnava traditions worship Radha as the eternal consort and wedded wife of Krishna. In contrast, traditions like Gaudiya Vaishnavism revere her as Krishna's lover and the divine consort.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nimbarka dan banyak tradisi Waisnawa memujanya sebagai istri abadi Krishna.",
        "en": "Nimbarka and many Vaishnava traditions worship her as Krishna's eternal wife."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c06",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "Radha accompanies Krishna in all his incarnations. The Nimbarka Sampradaya and many other Vaishnava traditions worship Radha as the eternal consort and wedded wife of Krishna. In contrast, traditions like Gaudiya Vaishnavism revere her as Krishna's lover and the divine consort.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tradisi Gaudiya Waisnawa menekankan Radha sebagai kekasih serta pasangan ilahi Krishna.",
        "en": "Gaudiya Vaishnava tradition emphasises Radha as Krishna's beloved and divine consort."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c07",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "In Radha Vallabha Sampradaya and Haridasi Sampradaya, only Radha is worshipped as the Supreme being. Radha is described as the chief of Braj Gopis (milkmaids of Braj) and queen of Goloka and Braj including Vrindavan and Barsana. She has inspired numerous literary works, and her Raslila dance with Krishna has inspired many types of performance arts.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Radha Vallabha dan Haridasi memujanya sebagai sosok tertinggi.",
        "en": "Radha Vallabha and Haridasi venerate her as the supreme being."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c08",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "In Radha Vallabha Sampradaya and Haridasi Sampradaya, only Radha is worshipped as the Supreme being. Radha is described as the chief of Braj Gopis (milkmaids of Braj) and queen of Goloka and Braj including Vrindavan and Barsana. She has inspired numerous literary works, and her Raslila dance with Krishna has inspired many types of performance arts.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut pemimpin para gopi Braj dan ratu Goloka serta Braj.",

    {

      "quote": "The Sanskrit term Rādhā (Sanskrit: राधा) means \"prosperity, success, perfection and wealth\". It is a common word and name found in various contexts in the ancient and medieval texts of India. The word appears in the Vedic literature as well as the Hindu epics, but is elusive. The name also appears for a figure in the epic Mahabharata. Rādhikā refers to an endearing form of Radha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Radha dijelaskan bermakna kemakmuran, keberhasilan, kesempurnaan, dan kekayaan.",
        "en": "Radha's name is explained as prosperity, success, perfection, and wealth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c10",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "The fifth chapter, Fifth night of Narada Pancharatra mentioned 1008 names of Radha under the title Shri Radha Saharsnama Strotam. The 68th chapter, Tritiya paad of Narada Purana also listed 500 names of Radha. Some of the common names and epithets are:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Narada Pancharatra mencatat 1008 namanya dalam Shri Radha Saharsnama Strotam.",
        "en": "The Narada Pancharatra records 1008 of her names in the Shri Radha Saharsnama Strotam."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c11",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "During the Bhakti movement era in the sixteenth century, she became more well known as her extraordinary love for Krishna was highlighted.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada gerakan Bhakti abad keenam belas, cinta Radha kepada Krishna menjadi lebih menonjol.",
        "en": "In the sixteenth century Bhakti movement, Radha's love for Krishna became more prominent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c12",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "Radha's first major appearance in the 12th-century Gita Govinda in Sanskrit by Jayadeva, as well as Nimbarkacharya's philosophical works. Thus in the Gita Govinda Krishna speaks to Radha:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c13",
      "source_id": "radha-s1",
      "quote": "According to Jaya Chemburkar, there are at least two significant and different aspects of Radha in the literature associated with her, such as Sri Radhika namasahasram. One aspect is she is a milkmaid (gopi), another as a female deity similar to those found in the Hindu goddess traditions.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 17",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kajian yang diringkas artikel membedakan Radha sebagai gopi dan sebagai dewi.",
        "en": "Scholarship summarised by the article distinguishes Radha as a gopi and as a goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c14",
      "source_id": "radha-s2",
      "quote": "Its subject\r\nis the estrangement of Radha and Krishna caused by Krishna's love\r\nfor other cowgirls, Radha's anguish at Krishna's neglect and lastly\r\nthe rapture which attends their final reunion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 173",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kajian Archer menyebut Gita Govinda menuturkan perpisahan, kerinduan, dan pertemuan kembali Radha dengan Krishna.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "radha-c15",
      "source_id": "radha-s3",
      "quote": "Beautiful Radha, Jasmine bosomed Radha,\nAll in the Spring-time waited by the wood\nFor Krishna fair, Krishna the all-forgetful,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Naga adalah kelompok makhluk ilahi atau setengah ilahi berbentuk manusia dan ular dalam tradisi Asia. Tempat tinggalnya disebut Patala, alam bawah.",
      "en": "Nagas are a class of divine or semidivine human and serpent beings in Asian traditions. Their dwelling is called Patala, the netherworld.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "naga-c01",
        "naga-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka dapat tampil sebagai manusia, ular, atau makhluk campuran manusia dan ular. Naga perempuan disebut Nagini. Kashyapa dan Kadru disebut sebagai orang tua kelompok ini dalam legenda. Nagaraja adalah gelar raja para naga. Kisah mereka penting dalam tradisi Hindu dan Buddha di Asia Selatan serta Tenggara. Naga dalam Sanskerta terutama berarti ular, sering kali kobra India. Naga menonjol dalam ikonografi Hindu dan buku pertama Mahabharata. Alam Naga-loka digambarkan berisi permata, emas, dan harta bumi. Naga dikaitkan dengan sungai, danau, laut, dan sumur serta peran penjaga harta. Vishnu digambarkan berbaring pada Shesha atau dilindungi tudungnya. Di India Selatan, sarang rayap dianggap tempat tinggal nagamma perempuan. Mackenzie membahas naga sebagai setengah dewa yang kadang membantu manusia dan memberi minuman penambah kekuatan dalam cerita. Museum Cleveland menjelaskan pentingnya naga bagi pemuja lokal sebagai pengendali air yang menunjang kemakmuran.",
      "en": "They can appear as humans, serpents, or mixed human and serpent beings. A female naga is called Nagini. Kashyapa and Kadru are called this class's parents in legend. Nagaraja is the title of the king of nagas. Their narratives are important in Hindu and Buddhist traditions in South and Southeast Asia. Naga in Sanskrit primarily means snake, often the Indian cobra. Nagas are prominent in Hindu iconography and the first book of the Mahabharata. Naga-loka is described as containing gems, gold, and earthly treasures. Nagas are associated with rivers, lakes, seas, and wells and with guarding treasure. Vishnu is depicted reclining on Shesha or sheltered by his hood. In South India, termite hills are regarded as dwellings of female nagammas. Mackenzie discusses nagas as demigods who sometimes aid humans and give strength enhancing drinks in stories.       "quote": "Nagaraja is the title given to the king of the nāgas.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah mereka penting dalam tradisi Hindu dan Buddha di Asia Selatan serta Tenggara.",

    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c08",
      "source_id": "naga-s1",
      "quote": "In Sanskrit, a nāga (नाग) primarily refers to a snake, most often specifically the Indian cobra (Naja naja). However, the term is also used generically to denote any snake, sharing this broader meaning with other common Sanskrit synonyms such as sarpa (सर्प) and phaṇin (फणिन्).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga dalam Sanskerta terutama berarti ular, sering kali kobra India.",
        "en": "Naga in Sanskrit primarily means snake, often the Indian cobra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c09",
      "source_id": "naga-s1",
      "quote": "Nāgas, as a serpent-shaped group of deities that often take form as cobras, are prominent in Hindu iconography, throughout Hindu texts (especially in the first book of the Mahābhārata) and in local folk traditions of worship.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga menonjol dalam ikonografi Hindu dan buku pertama Mahabharata.",
        "en": "Nagas are prominent in Hindu iconography and the first book of the Mahabharata."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c10",
      "source_id": "naga-s1",
      "quote": "Their domain is in the enchanted underworld, the underground realm filled with gems, gold and other earthly treasures called Naga-loka or Patala-loka.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alam Naga-loka digambarkan berisi permata, emas, dan harta bumi.",
        "en": "Naga-loka is described as containing gems, gold, and earthly treasures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c11",
      "source_id": "naga-s1",
      "quote": "They are also often associated with bodies of waters—including rivers, lakes, seas, and wells—and are guardians of treasure.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga dikaitkan dengan sungai, danau, laut, dan sumur serta peran penjaga harta.",
        "en": "Nagas are associated with rivers, lakes, seas, and wells and with guarding treasure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c12",
      "source_id": "naga-s1",
      "quote": "Vishnu is originally portrayed in the form sheltered by Sheshanāga or reclining on Shesha, but the iconography has been extended to other deities as well.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vishnu digambarkan berbaring pada Shesha atau dilindungi tudungnya.",
        "en": "Vishnu is depicted reclining on Shesha or sheltered by his hood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c13",
      "source_id": "naga-s1",
      "quote": "In South India, termite hills are believed to be the dwelling place of female nagammas, whereas Himalayan Nags and Naginis, such as Naiṇī Devī of Pindar Valley, are worshipped as underworld beings protecting water resources and the wellbeing of village and valley.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di India Selatan, sarang rayap dianggap tempat tinggal nagamma perempuan.",
        "en": "In South India, termite hills are regarded as dwellings of female nagammas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "naga-c14",
      "source_id": "naga-s2",
      "quote": "When serpent worship became prevalent among the\r\nAryans, the Nagas were regarded as demigods. Their city was the\r\nParadise of serpent worshippers. The female Nagas\r\nwere beautiful nymphs, who were sometimes wooed by\r\nmortals.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Deva adalah istilah Sanskerta untuk dewa atau makhluk ilahi dalam agama Hindu. Deva bersifat maskulin, dengan bentuk feminin Devi.",
      "en": "Deva is a Sanskrit term for a god or divine being in Hinduism. Deva is masculine, with the feminine form Devi.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "deva-hinduism-c01",
        "deva-hinduism-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Maknanya mencakup bersinar, luhur, dan makhluk surgawi. Literatur Weda awal menggunakan Deva dan Asura untuk makhluk gaib, dengan pemaknaan yang berubah kemudian. Dalam teks pasca-Weda, deva mewakili kebaikan dan asura keburukan. Sebagian karya abad pertengahan juga memakai nama Sura untuk deva. Deva termasuk dalam teori kosmologi Hindu bersama kelompok makhluk lain. Samhita menyebut 33 deva dengan pembagian sebelas pada masing-masing dari tiga dunia. Lapisan Brahmana memakai pembagian 12 Aditya, 11 Rudra, 8 Vasu, dan 2 Aswin. Sebagian deva mewakili kekuatan alam, sebagian nilai moral seperti Varuna dan Mitra. Indra, Agni, dan Soma termasuk deva yang sering disebut Rigveda. Sekumpulan dewa dapat dipuja bersama sebagai Vishvedeva. Max Muller menafsirkan himne yang meninggikan masing-masing deva sebagai henoteisme. Mackenzie mengutip kisah yang membedakan deva memilih kebenaran dan asura memilih kebohongan. Rigveda 1.139.11 mengundang sebelas dewa di langit, sebelas di bumi, dan sebelas di air menerima pengorbanan.",
      "en": "Its meanings include shining, exalted, and heavenly being. Early Vedic literature uses Deva and Asura for supernatural beings, with meanings that later change. In post Vedic texts, devas represent good and asuras evil. Some medieval works also use Sura as a name for devas. Devas occur in Hindu cosmological theories alongside other classes of beings. The Samhitas describe 33 devas divided into eleven in each of three worlds. The Brahmana layer uses a division of 12 Adityas, 11 Rudras, 8 Vasus, and 2 Asvins. Some devas represent natural forces and others moral values, such as Varuna and Mitra. Indra, Agni, and Soma are among the devas frequently mentioned in the Rigveda. A group of gods can be worshipped together as the Vishvedevas. Max Muller interprets hymns exalting each deva in turn as henotheism. Mackenzie quotes an account distinguishing devas choosing truth from asuras choosing falsehood. Rigveda 1.139.11 invites eleven gods in heaven, eleven on earth, and eleven in the waters to accept sacrifice.",

    "id": "Apsara adalah kelompok makhluk perempuan surgawi dalam tradisi Hindu dan Buddha.",
    "en": "Apsaras are a class of celestial female beings in Hindu and Buddhist traditions.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "apsara-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Apsara adalah kelompok makhluk perempuan surgawi dalam tradisi Hindu dan Buddha. Mereka dikenal sebagai penari istana Indra.",
      "en": "Apsaras are a class of celestial female beings in Hindu and Buddhist traditions. They are known as dancers at Indra's court.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apsara-c01",
        "apsara-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gandharva berhubungan dengan mereka sebagai musisi dan pasangan. Sebagian kisah memberi mereka kemampuan mengubah wujud. Indra mengirim apsara menggoda resi yang bertapa dalam sejumlah cerita. Urvashi, Menaka, Rambha, Tilottama, dan Ghritachi termasuk apsara yang dikenal. Di Jepang, nama Tennin dan Tennyo digunakan untuk sosok terkait apsara. Etimologi Monier-Williams menghubungkan namanya dengan bergerak di air atau di antara air awan. Uraian awal membandingkan apsara dengan nimfa air. Rigveda memuat dialog Urvashi dengan kekasih manusia Pururavas. Kaushitaki Upanishad mengaitkan apsara dengan minyak wangi, rangkaian bunga, pakaian, dan wewangian bubuk. Ramayana dan Purana menghubungkan asal apsara dengan pengadukan lautan. Mahabharata menuturkan Tilottama menyelamatkan dunia dari Sunda dan Upasunda. Mackenzie menuturkan apsara menari ketika Gandharva memainkan alat musik ilahi.",
      "en": "Gandharvas are associated with them as musicians and partners. Some accounts give them the ability to change shape. Indra sends apsaras to tempt ascetic sages in several stories. Urvashi, Menaka, Rambha, Tilottama, and Ghritachi are among the known apsaras. In Japan, the names Tennin and Tennyo are used for figures associated with apsaras. Monier Williams's etymology connects the name with moving in water or among cloud waters. Early descriptions compare apsaras to water nymphs. The Rigveda contains Urvashi's dialogue with her mortal lover Pururavas. The Kaushitaki Upanishad associates apsaras with ointments, garlands, clothes, and powdered aromatics. The Ramayana and Puranas connect the origin of apsaras with the churning of the ocean. The Mahabharata recounts Tilottama saving the world from Sunda and Upasunda. Mackenzie recounts apsaras dancing when Gandharvas play divine instruments.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "apsara-c01",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "Apsaras (Sanskrit: अप्सरस्, IAST: Apsaras, Khmer: អប្សរា, Pali: अच्छरा, romanized: Accharā, Thai: อัปสร) are a class of celestial beings in Hindu and Buddhist culture. They were originally a type of female spirit associated with clouds and water, but, later came to play the role of a \"nymph\" or \"fairy\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apsara adalah kelompok makhluk perempuan surgawi dalam tradisi Hindu dan Buddha.",
        "en": "Apsaras are a class of celestial female beings in Hindu and Buddhist traditions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c02",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The apsaras are described as being beautiful, youthful and elegant, and are said to be able to change their shape at will; making anyone fall for their beauty. There are two types of apsaras—laukika (worldly) and daivika (divine). They are said to excel at dancing, and are often considered the wives of the gandharvas, the court musicians of Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dikenal sebagai penari istana Indra.",
        "en": "They are known as dancers at Indra's court."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c03",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The apsaras are described as being beautiful, youthful and elegant, and are said to be able to change their shape at will; making anyone fall for their beauty. There are two types of apsaras—laukika (worldly) and daivika (divine). They are said to excel at dancing, and are often considered the wives of the gandharvas, the court musicians of Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gandharva berhubungan dengan mereka sebagai musisi dan pasangan.",
        "en": "Gandharvas are associated with them as musicians and partners."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c04",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The apsaras are described as being beautiful, youthful and elegant, and are said to be able to change their shape at will; making anyone fall for their beauty. There are two types of apsaras—laukika (worldly) and daivika (divine). They are said to excel at dancing, and are often considered the wives of the gandharvas, the court musicians of Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kisah memberi mereka kemampuan mengubah wujud.",
        "en": "Some accounts give them the ability to change shape."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c05",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The apsaras are described as being beautiful, youthful and elegant, and are said to be able to change their shape at will; making anyone fall for their beauty. There are two types of apsaras—laukika (worldly) and daivika (divine). They are said to excel at dancing, and are often considered the wives of the gandharvas, the court musicians of Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Indra mengirim apsara menggoda resi yang bertapa dalam sejumlah cerita.",
        "en": "Indra sends apsaras to tempt ascetic sages in several stories."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c06",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The apsaras are described as being beautiful, youthful and elegant, and are said to be able to change their shape at will; making anyone fall for their beauty. There are two types of apsaras—laukika (worldly) and daivika (divine). They are said to excel at dancing, and are often considered the wives of the gandharvas, the court musicians of Indra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Urvashi, Menaka, Rambha, Tilottama, dan Ghritachi termasuk apsara yang dikenal.",
        "en": "Urvashi, Menaka, Rambha, Tilottama, and Ghritachi are among the known apsaras."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c07",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "In Japan, Apsara are known as \"Tennin\" (天人); \"Tennyo\" (天女) for \"female Tennin\" and \"Tennan\" (天男) for \"male Tennin\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Jepang, nama Tennin dan Tennyo digunakan untuk sosok terkait apsara.",
        "en": "In Japan, the names Tennin and Tennyo are used for figures associated with apsaras."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c08",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The Monier-Williams et al. (1899) gives the etymology as अप् + √सृ, \"going in the waters or between the waters of the clouds\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Etimologi Monier-Williams menghubungkan namanya dengan bergerak di air atau di antara air awan.",
        "en": "Monier Williams's etymology connects the name with moving in water or among cloud waters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c09",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The most ancient descriptions of apsara portray them as \"water nymph\"-like beings.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Uraian awal membandingkan apsara dengan nimfa air.",
        "en": "Early descriptions compare apsaras to water nymphs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c10",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The Rigveda tells of an apsara who is the wife of Gandharva; however, the Rigveda also seems to allow for the existence of more than one apsara.\nThe only apsara specifically named is Urvashi.  An entire hymn deals with the colloquy between Urvashi and her mortal lover Pururavas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda memuat dialog Urvashi dengan kekasih manusia Pururavas.",
        "en": "The Rigveda contains Urvashi's dialogue with her mortal lover Pururavas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c11",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The Kaushitaki Upanishad mentions apsaras as a class of divinities associated with ointments, garlands, vestments, and powdered aromatics.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 8",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaushitaki Upanishad mengaitkan apsara dengan minyak wangi, rangkaian bunga, pakaian, dan wewangian bubuk.",
        "en": "The Kaushitaki Upanishad associates apsaras with ointments, garlands, clothes, and powdered aromatics."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c12",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The origin of the apsaras is described in the Ramayana and Puranas. Apsaras are further associated with water by relating them to the churning of the ocean, water sports, and groups such as nāgas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ramayana dan Purana menghubungkan asal apsara dengan pengadukan lautan.",
        "en": "The Ramayana and Puranas connect the origin of apsaras with the churning of the ocean."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c13",
      "source_id": "apsara-s1",
      "quote": "The Mahabharata documents the exploits of individual apsaras, such as Tilottama, who rescued the world from the rampaging asura brothers Sunda and Upasunda; and Urvashi, who attempted to seduce the hero Arjuna.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mahabharata menuturkan Tilottama menyelamatkan dunia dari Sunda dan Upasunda.",
        "en": "The Mahabharata recounts Tilottama saving the world from Sunda and Upasunda."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c14",
      "source_id": "apsara-s2",
      "quote": "When they play on their divine instruments the fairy-like\r\nApsaras, who are all females, dance merrily.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 471",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan apsara menari ketika Gandharva memainkan alat musik ilahi.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts apsaras dancing when Gandharvas play divine instruments."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apsara-c15",
      "source_id": "apsara-s3",
      "quote": "Apsaras c. 1920 France or Cambodia ... This sculpture has been carved in the style of the reliefs depicting celestial maidens at the \"Terrace of the Leper King\" in Angkor Thom.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Koleksi Cleveland mencakup patung Apsaras sekitar 1920 yang mengikuti gaya relief bidadari surgawi Angkor Thom.",
        "en": "The Cleveland collection includes an Apsaras sculpture around 1920 following the style of Angkor Thom celestial maiden reliefs."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Devi adalah istilah Sanskerta untuk dewi dalam agama India. Pasangan istilah maskulinnya adalah Deva.",
      "en": "Devi is the Sanskrit term for a goddess in Indian religions. Its masculine counterpart is Deva.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "devi-c01",
        "devi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tradisi Shaktisme berpusat pada Devi. Literatur Purana abad pertengahan memperluas kisah Devi, termasuk Devi Mahatmya. Devi Mahatmya memujanya sebagai kebenaran tertinggi dan daya utama. Devi dan Deva dijelaskan mengacu pada yang ilahi, luhur, dan bersinar. Bhagavati menjadi sebutan kehormatan dewi seperti Lakshmi dan Durga. Artikel menghubungkan Devisukta dengan Rigveda 10.125. Weda menyebut Prithvi, Aditi, Vac, Ratri, dan Aranyani sebagai sejumlah dewi kosmis. Dalam tradisi Shakta Devi adalah sosok tertinggi; tradisi Smarta memasukkannya dalam lima bentuk utama Brahman. Tradisi lain menekankan daya aktif dewi yang melengkapi dewa pasangan. Devi Upanishad mengajarkan Shakti sebagai Brahman serta asal materi dan kesadaran. Dalam Devi Upanishad, Devi menyatakan dirinya pencipta bumi dan langit serta kesadaran tak terbatas. WHE menjelaskan sisi Uma yang ramah dan sisi Durga yang menakutkan sebagai penggambaran Devi berbeda. Pembicara perempuan dalam Rigveda 10.125 menyebut dirinya Ratu dan penghimpun harta yang layak dipuja.",
      "en": "Shaktism centres on Devi. Medieval Puranic literature expands Devi narratives, including the Devi Mahatmya. The Devi Mahatmya reveres her as ultimate truth and supreme power. Devi and Deva are explained as referring to the divine, exalted, and shining. Bhagavati is an honorific for goddesses such as Lakshmi and Durga. The article connects the Devisukta with Rigveda 10.125. The Vedas name Prithvi, Aditi, Vac, Ratri, and Aranyani among cosmic goddesses. In Shakta tradition Devi is supreme; Smarta tradition includes her among five primary forms of Brahman. Other traditions emphasise the goddess's active power complementing her divine partner. The Devi Upanishad teaches Shakti as Brahman and the origin of matter and consciousness. In the Devi Upanishad, Devi declares herself creator of earth and heaven and infinite consciousness.       "quote": "The Vedas name numerous cosmic goddesses such as Prithvi (earth), Aditi (cosmic moral order), Vāc (sound), Nirṛti (destruction), Ratri (night) and Aranyani (forest); bounty goddesses such as Dinsana, Raka, Puramdhi, Parendi, Bharati and Mahi are among others are mentioned in the Rigveda.       "id": "devi-c12",
      "source_id": "devi-s1",
      "quote": "Devi-inspired philosophy is propounded in many Hindu texts such as the Devi Upanishad, which teaches that Shakti is essentially Brahman (ultimate metaphysical Reality) and that from her arises prakṛti (matter) and purusha (consciousness) and that she is bliss and non-bliss, the Vedas and what is different from it, the born and the unborn and all of the universe.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Devi Upanishad mengajarkan Shakti sebagai Brahman serta asal materi dan kesadaran.",
        "en": "The Devi Upanishad teaches Shakti as Brahman and the origin of matter and consciousness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devi-c13",
      "source_id": "devi-s1",
      "quote": "Devi asserts that she is the creator of earth and heaven and resides there. ... She is, states Devi, the eternal and infinite consciousness engulfing earth and heaven, and 'all forms of bliss and non-bliss, knowledge and ignorance, Brahman and Non-Brahman'.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 13",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Devi Upanishad, Devi menyatakan dirinya pencipta bumi dan langit serta kesadaran tak terbatas.",
        "en": "In the Devi Upanishad, Devi declares herself creator of earth and heaven and infinite consciousness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devi-c14",
      "source_id": "devi-s2",
      "quote": "Devi's character has two opposing sides represented by various separate female deities: as Uma, the benevolent, and as Durga, the terrible.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "WHE menjelaskan sisi Uma yang ramah dan sisi Durga yang menakutkan sebagai penggambaran Devi berbeda.",
        "en": "WHE explains benevolent Uma and fearsome Durga as different depictions of Devi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "devi-c15",
      "source_id": "devi-s3",
      "quote": "I am the Queen, the gatherer-up of treasures, most thoughtful, first of those who merit worship.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pembicara perempuan dalam Rigveda 10.125 menyebut dirinya Ratu dan penghimpun harta yang layak dipuja.",
        "en": "The female speaker in Rigveda 10.125 calls herself Queen and gatherer of treasures worthy of worship."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "Aditi adalah dewi Weda penting dalam Hindu, dengan nama yang berarti tak terbatas atau tak terikat. Ia mempersonifikasikan keluasan kosmos.",
      "en": "Aditi is an important Vedic goddess in Hinduism, with a name meaning boundless or unbound. She personifies the vastness of the cosmos.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "aditi-c01",
        "aditi-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Ia dikaitkan dengan keibuan dan kesuburan. Aditi disebut ibu kelompok dewa Aditya. Artikel mencatat namanya disebut lebih dari 250 kali dalam Rigveda. Versi Purana menyebut Aditi putri Daksha dan Asikni serta pasangan Kashyapa. Dalam kisah itu Indra lahir setelah Aditi meminta seorang putra ideal. Versi Purana tersebut kemudian memberi dua belas Aditya sebagai putranya. Shatapatha Brahmana juga memanggil Aditi sebagai bumi dalam pengorbanan. Ia disebut ibu Vamana, penjelmaan Vishnu. Pemohon memanggil Aditi untuk perlindungan, keselamatan, dan kelimpahan. Penjelasan nama mengaitkan awalan penyangkal dengan akar da yang berarti mengikat. Himne memohon agar Aditi melepaskan pemohon dari belenggu, dosa, dan penderitaan. Versi yang dibahas Mackenzie memberi Aditi delapan putra dan menyebut putra kedelapan yang dibuang lalu dibentuk menjadi Vivasvat. Rigveda 1.89.10 mengidentifikasi Aditi dengan langit, ruang antara, ibu, ayah, dan putra.",
      "en": "She is associated with motherhood and fertility. Aditi is called the mother of the Aditya gods. The article records her name appearing more than 250 times in the Rigveda. A Puranic version describes Aditi as Daksha and Asikni's daughter and Kashyapa's consort. In that account Indra is born after Aditi asks for an ideal son. That Puranic version subsequently gives twelve Adityas as her sons. The Shatapatha Brahmana also invokes Aditi as earth in sacrifice. She is called Vamana's mother, an incarnation of Vishnu. Petitioners invoke Aditi for protection, safety, and abundance. An explanation of the name connects a negative prefix with the root da, meaning to bind. Hymns ask Aditi to release the petitioner from bonds, sin, and suffering. The version discussed by Mackenzie gives Aditi eight sons and describes the discarded eighth son later fashioned into Vivasvat. Rigveda 1.89.10 identifies Aditi with heaven, mid air, mother, father, and son.",

      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "aditi-c07",
      "source_id": "aditi-s1",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "aditi-c08",
      "source_id": "aditi-s1",
      "quote": "Later, Aditi gave birth to the twelve adityas, namely Varuna, Parjanya, Mitra, Amsha, Pushan, Dhatri, Aryaman, Surya, Bhaga, Savitr, Vamana, and Viṣṇu.",

      "id": "aditi-c09",
      "source_id": "aditi-s1",
      "quote": "According to the Shatapatha Brahmana (a commentary on the Shukla Yajurveda), Aditi is also invoked during ritual sacrificial offerings as being synonymous with the Earth:",

      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 9",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      "source_id": "aditi-s1",
      "quote": "In the Rigveda, Aditi is one of the most important figures of all. As a mothering presence, Aditi is often asked to guard the one who petitions her (Mandala 1.106.7; Mandala 8.18.6) or to provide him or her with wealth, safety, and abundance (Mandala 10.100; 1.94.15).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 10",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemohon memanggil Aditi untuk perlindungan, keselamatan, dan kelimpahan.",
        "en": "Petitioners invoke Aditi for protection, safety, and abundance."
      }
    },

      "source_id": "aditi-s1",

      "context": "etymology",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vayu adalah dewa angin Hindu dan utusan ilahi para dewa. Nama lainnya termasuk Vata dan Pavana.",
      "en": "Vayu is the Hindu wind god and divine messenger of the gods. His other names include Vata and Pavana.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vayu-c01",
        "vayu-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia berhubungan erat dengan Indra dalam kitab Weda. Ia disebut lahir dari napas Vishvapurusha. Ia menjadi dewa pertama yang minum Soma dalam uraian Weda. Upanishad memujinya sebagai Prana, napas hidup dunia. Kitab kemudian menyebutnya penjaga arah barat laut. Wiracarita menyebutnya ayah Hanuman dan Bhima. Vayu dijelaskan sebagai peniup dan Prana sebagai napas kehidupan. Rigveda menghubungkannya dengan kereta bercahaya yang ditarik kuda. Sebagai anggota delapan Vasu ia juga disebut Anila. Dalam Buddhisme Jepang ia termasuk Dua Belas Dewa penjaga arah. Nama Jepangnya adalah Futen. Mackenzie mengutip himne yang menghubungkan Vayu dengan dua kuda merah pada kereta serta kawanan rusa. Rigveda 1.2 memanggil Vayu untuk minum Soma yang dipersiapkan dan mendengar doa.",
      "en": "He is closely associated with Indra in Vedic scriptures. He is described as born from Vishvapurusha's breath. He is the first god to drink Soma in Vedic accounts. The Upanishads praise him as Prana, the world's life breath. Later scriptures describe him as guardian of the northwest. The epics describe him as Hanuman and Bhima's father. Vayu is explained as blower and Prana as the breath of life. The Rigveda connects him with a shining chariot drawn by horses. As a member of the eight Vasus he is also called Anila. In Japanese Buddhism he belongs to the Twelve Devas guarding directions. His Japanese name is Futen. Mackenzie quotes a hymn connecting Vayu with two red horses on a chariot and a team of deer. Rigveda 1.2 calls Vayu to drink the prepared Soma and hear the prayer.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vayu-c03",
        "vayu-c04",
        "vayu-c05",

      "publisher": "E. J. Lazarus and Co., 1896; Wikisource",
      "published": null,
