  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vayu-c01",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "is the Hindu god of the winds as well as the divine messenger of the gods. In the Vedic scriptures, Vayu is an important deity and is closely associated with Indra, the king of gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vayu adalah dewa angin Hindu dan utusan ilahi para dewa.",
        "en": "Vayu is the Hindu wind god and divine messenger of the gods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c02",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "Vayu (Sanskrit: वायु, romanised: Vāyu, lit. 'Wind/Air'; Sanskrit pronunciation: [ʋaːju]), also known as Vata (Sanskrit: वात, romanised: Vāta, lit. 'Wind/Air') and Pavana (Sanskrit: पवन, romanised: Pávana, lit. 'Purifier'), is the Hindu god of the winds as well as the divine messenger of the gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama lainnya termasuk Vata dan Pavana.",
        "en": "His other names include Vata and Pavana."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c03",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "Vayu (Sanskrit: वायु, romanised: Vāyu, lit. 'Wind/Air'; Sanskrit pronunciation: [ʋaːju]), also known as Vata (Sanskrit: वात, romanised: Vāta, lit. 'Wind/Air') and Pavana (Sanskrit: पवन, romanised: Pávana, lit. 'Purifier'), is the Hindu god of the winds as well as the divine messenger of the gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berhubungan erat dengan Indra dalam kitab Weda.",
        "en": "He is closely associated with Indra in Vedic scriptures."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c04",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "Vayu (Sanskrit: वायु, romanised: Vāyu, lit. 'Wind/Air'; Sanskrit pronunciation: [ʋaːju]), also known as Vata (Sanskrit: वात, romanised: Vāta, lit. 'Wind/Air') and Pavana (Sanskrit: पवन, romanised: Pávana, lit. 'Purifier'), is the Hindu god of the winds as well as the divine messenger of the gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut lahir dari napas Vishvapurusha.",
        "en": "He is described as born from Vishvapurusha's breath."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c05",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "Vayu (Sanskrit: वायु, romanised: Vāyu, lit. 'Wind/Air'; Sanskrit pronunciation: [ʋaːju]), also known as Vata (Sanskrit: वात, romanised: Vāta, lit. 'Wind/Air') and Pavana (Sanskrit: पवन, romanised: Pávana, lit. 'Purifier'), is the Hindu god of the winds as well as the divine messenger of the gods.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi dewa pertama yang minum Soma dalam uraian Weda.",
        "en": "He is the first god to drink Soma in Vedic accounts."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c06",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "The Upanishads praise him as Prana or 'life breath of the world'.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Upanishad memujinya sebagai Prana, napas hidup dunia.",
        "en": "The Upanishads praise him as Prana, the world's life breath."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c07",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "In the later Hindu scriptures, he is described as a dikpala (one of the guardians of the direction), who looks over the north-west direction.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab kemudian menyebutnya penjaga arah barat laut.",
        "en": "Later scriptures describe him as guardian of the northwest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c08",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "The Hindu epics describe him as the father of the god Hanuman and Bhima.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wiracarita menyebutnya ayah Hanuman dan Bhima.",
        "en": "The epics describe him as Hanuman and Bhima's father."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c09",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "The word for air (vāyu) or wind (pavana) is one of the classical elements in Hinduism. The Sanskrit word Vāta literally means 'blown'; Vāyu, 'blower' and Prāna, 'breathing' (viz. the breath of life, cf. the *an- in animate).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vayu dijelaskan sebagai peniup dan Prana sebagai napas kehidupan.",
        "en": "Vayu is explained as blower and Prana as the breath of life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c10",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "In the hymns, Vayu is 'described as having \"exceptional beauty\" and moving noisily in his shining coach, driven by two or forty-nine or one-thousand white and purple horses.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda menghubungkannya dengan kereta bercahaya yang ditarik kuda.",
        "en": "The Rigveda connects him with a shining chariot drawn by horses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c11",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "Vayu is also one of the Vasus, a group of eight deities mentioned in Ramayana, Mahabharata and Vedas. Within this categorisation, Vayu is also known as Anila.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai anggota delapan Vasu ia juga disebut Anila.",
        "en": "As a member of the eight Vasus he is also called Anila."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c12",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "In Japanese Buddhism, especially Mikkyō traditions such as Shingon, he is usually classified as one of the Twelve Devas (Japanese: 十二天, romanised: Jūniten) grouped together as directional guardians. He presides over the northwest direction.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 11",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Buddhisme Jepang ia termasuk Dua Belas Dewa penjaga arah.",
        "en": "In Japanese Buddhism he belongs to the Twelve Devas guarding directions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c13",
      "source_id": "vayu-s1",
      "quote": "In Japan, he is called Fūten (風天). He is included with the other eleven devas, which include Taishakuten (Śakra/Indra), Katen (Agni), Enmaten (Yama), Rasetsuten (Nirṛti/Rākṣasa), Ishanaten (Īśāna), Bishamonten (Vaiśravaṇa/Kubera), Suiten (Varuṇa), Bonten (Brahmā), Jiten (Pṛthivī), Nitten (Sūrya/Āditya) and Gatten (Candra).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 12",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Jepangnya adalah Futen.",
        "en": "His Japanese name is Futen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c14",
      "source_id": "vayu-s2",
      "quote": "The Indian Vata is invoked, as Vayu, in a beautiful\r\npassage in one of the hymns which refers to his “two\r\nred horses yoked to the chariot”: he had also, like the\r\nMaruts, a team of deer. The poet calls to the wind:",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 228",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mengutip himne yang menghubungkan Vayu dengan dua kuda merah pada kereta serta kawanan rusa.",
        "en": "Mackenzie quotes a hymn connecting Vayu with two red horses on a chariot and a team of deer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vayu-c15",
      "source_id": "vayu-s3",
      "quote": "BEAUTIFUL Vayu, come, for thee these Soma drops have been prepared:\n     Drink of them, hearken to our call.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda 1.2 memanggil Vayu untuk minum Soma yang dipersiapkan dan mendengar doa.",
        "en": "Rigveda 1.2 calls Vayu to drink the prepared Soma and hear the prayer."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Warna kuda pada kereta",
        "en": "Chariot horse colours"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Kuda putih dan ungu dalam ringkasan himne.",
            "en": "White and purple horses in the hymn summary."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "vayu-c10"
          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Dua kuda merah dalam kutipan Mackenzie.",
            "en": "Two red horses in Mackenzie's quotation."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "vayu-c14"
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Ilustrasi dalam artikel tidak dipakai tanpa pemeriksaan berkas Commons, kecocokan subjek, dan lisensinya; belum ada gambar yang terverifikasi untuk entri ini."

}

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sati adalah dewi Hindu kebahagiaan perkawinan dan umur panjang, serta aspek Shakti. Ia merupakan istri pertama Shiva; Parvati dipandang sebagai kelahirannya kembali.",
      "en": "Sati is the Hindu goddess of marital felicity and longevity and an aspect of Shakti. She is Shiva's first wife; Parvati is regarded as her reincarnation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sati-c01",
        "sati-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda, ia menikahi Shiva bertentangan dengan keinginan ayahnya, Daksha. Bagian tubuh Sati yang jatuh ke bumi dikaitkan dengan tempat suci Shakti pitha. Nama Sati dijelaskan berarti benar, berbudi, atau mulia dalam bahasa Sanskerta. Mackenzie menuturkan Sati terjun ke api pengorbanan setelah ayahnya menghina suaminya.",
      "en": "In legend she marries Shiva against her father Daksha's wishes. Sati's body parts falling to earth are associated with sacred Shakti pithas.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sati-c01",
      "source_id": "sati-s1",
      "quote": "is the Hindu goddess of marital felicity and longevity, and is worshipped as an aspect of the mother goddess Shakti.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sati adalah dewi Hindu kebahagiaan perkawinan dan umur panjang, serta aspek Shakti.",
        "en": "Sati is the Hindu goddess of marital felicity and longevity and an aspect of Shakti."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sati-c02",
      "source_id": "sati-s1",
      "quote": "Sati was the first wife of Shiva, the other being Parvati, who was Sati's reincarnation after her death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan istri pertama Shiva; Parvati dipandang sebagai kelahirannya kembali.",
        "en": "She is Shiva's first wife; Parvati is regarded as her reincarnation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sati-c03",
      "source_id": "sati-s1",
      "quote": "Legends describe Sati as the favourite child of Daksha, who marries Shiva against her father's wishes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda, ia menikahi Shiva bertentangan dengan keinginan ayahnya, Daksha.",
        "en": "In legend she marries Shiva against her father Daksha's wishes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sati-c04",
      "source_id": "sati-s1",
      "quote": "his Sudarshana Chakra which divided Sati's body parts falling on the ground at 51 different places. These places are now known as Shakti pithas, and they are sacred to Hindus.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagian tubuh Sati yang jatuh ke bumi dikaitkan dengan tempat suci Shakti pitha.",
        "en": "Sati's body parts falling to earth are associated with sacred Shakti pithas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sati-c05",
      "source_id": "sati-s1",
      "quote": "The name \"Satī\" means \"truthful\", \"virtuous\" or \"noble\" in Sanskrit. The word is derived from the \"sat\" which means \"truth\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Sati dijelaskan berarti benar, berbudi, atau mulia dalam bahasa Sanskerta.",
        "en": "The name Sati is explained as truthful, virtuous, or noble in Sanskrit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sati-c06",
      "source_id": "sati-s2",
      "quote": "As Sati, Shiva's wife is the ideal of a true and virtuous\r\nHindu woman. When Sati's husband was slighted by\r\nher father, the Deva-rishi, Daksha, she cast herself on\r\nthe sacrificial fire. Widows who died on the funeral\r\npyres of their husbands were called Sati[184], because in performing\r\nthis rite they imitated the faithful goddess.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 823",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan Sati terjun ke api pengorbanan setelah ayahnya menghina suaminya.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts Sati entering the sacrificial fire after her father slighted her husband."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Yaksha merupakan kelompok roh alam dalam mitologi Hindu yang berkaitan dengan air, pepohonan, hutan, harta, dan kesuburan.",
    "en": "Yakshas are a class of nature spirits in Hindu mythology associated with water, trees, forests, treasure, and fertility.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "yaksha-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Yaksha merupakan kelompok roh alam dalam mitologi Hindu yang berkaitan dengan air, pepohonan, hutan, harta, dan kesuburan. Bentuk perempuan disebut yakshi atau yakshini.",
      "en": "Yakshas are a class of nature spirits in Hindu mythology associated with water, trees, forests, treasure, and fertility. The female form is called yakshi or yakshini.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yaksha-c01",
        "yaksha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi yaksha dapat berupa roh alam yang tidak berbahaya atau hantu yang menghadang dan memangsa pelancong. Kubera dipandang sebagai raja para yaksha dalam Hindu, Buddha, dan Jain. Dalam literatur Buddhis, yaksha menjadi pengiring Vaisravana, penjaga arah utara. Mackenzie menyebut yaksha sebagai penjaga harta tersembunyi yang berkaitan dengan Kuvera.",
      "en": "A yaksha may be a harmless nature spirit or a ghost that waylays and devours travellers. Kubera is regarded as king of yakshas in Hinduism, Buddhism, and Jainism. In Buddhist literature yakshas attend Vaisravana, guardian of the northern quarter. Mackenzie calls yakshas guardians of hidden treasure associated with Kuvera.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "yaksha-c03",
        "yaksha-c04",

      "title": "The Project Gutenberg eBook of Indian Myth and Legend, by Donald A. Mackenzie.",
      "author": "Donald A. Mackenzie",
      "publisher": "The Gresham Publishing Company; Project Gutenberg",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "folklore-collection",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "yaksha-c01",
      "source_id": "yaksha-s1",
      "quote": "are a broad class of nature spirits in Hindu mythology which are associated with water, trees, forests, wilderness, treasure, and fertility.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Yaksha merupakan kelompok roh alam dalam mitologi Hindu yang berkaitan dengan air, pepohonan, hutan, harta, dan kesuburan.",
        "en": "Yakshas are a class of nature spirits in Hindu mythology associated with water, trees, forests, treasure, and fertility."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yaksha-c02",
      "source_id": "yaksha-s1",
      "quote": "The feminine form of the word is IAST: Yakṣī or Yakshini",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk perempuan disebut yakshi atau yakshini.",
        "en": "The female form is called yakshi or yakshini."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yaksha-c03",
      "source_id": "yaksha-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu, Jain and Buddhist texts, the yakṣas have a dual personality. A yakṣa may be an inoffensive nature spirit associated with woods and mountains, or a darker version of the yakṣa which is a kind of Bhoota (ghost) that haunts the wilderness and waylays and devours travellers, similar to the rakṣasas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi yaksha dapat berupa roh alam yang tidak berbahaya atau hantu yang menghadang dan memangsa pelancong.",
        "en": "A yaksha may be a harmless nature spirit or a ghost that waylays and devours travellers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yaksha-c04",
      "source_id": "yaksha-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu, Buddhist and Jain Religion, Kubera, the god of wealth and prosperity, is considered the king of the yakshas and protector of the world (Lokapāla). In Buddhism, he is equated with Vaiśravaṇa.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kubera dipandang sebagai raja para yaksha dalam Hindu, Buddha, dan Jain.",
        "en": "Kubera is regarded as king of yakshas in Hinduism, Buddhism, and Jainism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yaksha-c05",
      "source_id": "yaksha-s1",
      "quote": "In Buddhist literature, the yakṣa are the attendants of Vaiśravaṇa, the guardian of the northern quarter, a beneficent god who protects the righteous. The term also refers to the Twelve Heavenly Generals who guard Bhaiṣajyaguru, the Medicine Buddha. The yakshas of many Buddhist stories are ugly ogres, reborn in that form because of sins committed during their past lives as humans.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam literatur Buddhis, yaksha menjadi pengiring Vaisravana, penjaga arah utara.",
        "en": "In Buddhist literature yakshas attend Vaisravana, guardian of the northern quarter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "yaksha-c06",
      "source_id": "yaksha-s2",
      "quote": "They are guardians of hidden\r\ntreasure, like the dwarfs and giants of Teutonic legend,\r\nbeing associated with Kuvera, god of wealth, whose abode\r\nis situated among the Himalayan mountains.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 468",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menyebut yaksha sebagai penjaga harta tersembunyi yang berkaitan dengan Kuvera.",
        "en": "Mackenzie calls yakshas guardians of hidden treasure associated with Kuvera."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Kamadeva adalah dewa Hindu cinta, ketertarikan, kesenangan, dan keindahan. Ia digambarkan sebagai pemuda dengan busur tebu dan panah bunga.",
      "en": "Kamadeva is the Hindu god of love, attraction, pleasure, and beauty. He is depicted as a young man with a sugarcane bow and flower arrows.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kamadeva-c01",
        "kamadeva-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pasangannya adalah Rati. Versi Purana umumnya menyebutnya putra Brahma yang lahir dari pikiran. Kisahnya mencakup pembakaran oleh mata ketiga Shiva dan kelahiran kembali sebagai Pradyumna. Versi Mackenzie menyebut Kamadeva sebagai putra Vishnu dan Lakshmi.",
      "en": "His consort is Rati. Puranic accounts generally call him Brahma's mind born son. His story includes incineration by Shiva's third eye and rebirth as Pradyumna. Mackenzie's version calls Kamadeva the son of Vishnu and Lakshmi.",

    {
      "id": "kamadeva-c02",
      "source_id": "kamadeva-s1",
      "quote": "He is depicted as a handsome young man decked with ornaments and flowers, armed with a bow of sugarcane and shooting arrows of flowers.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

    {
      "id": "kamadeva-c03",
      "source_id": "kamadeva-s1",
      "quote": "He is often portrayed alongside his consort and female counterpart, Rati.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pasangannya adalah Rati.",
        "en": "His consort is Rati."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamadeva-c04",
      "source_id": "kamadeva-s1",
      "quote": "In the Puranas, Kamadeva is generally mentioned as a manasaputra (mind-born son) of the creator god Brahma. His most popular myth is his incineration by the god Shiva's third eye and rebirth on earth as Pradyumna, the eldest son of Krishna and his chief consort Rukmini.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Purana umumnya menyebutnya putra Brahma yang lahir dari pikiran.",
        "en": "Puranic accounts generally call him Brahma's mind born son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kamadeva-c05",
      "source_id": "kamadeva-s1",
      "quote": "In the Puranas, Kamadeva is generally mentioned as a manasaputra (mind-born son) of the creator god Brahma. His most popular myth is his incineration by the god Shiva's third eye and rebirth on earth as Pradyumna, the eldest son of Krishna and his chief consort Rukmini.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      "id": "Kubera adalah dewa kekayaan dan raja yaksha dalam Hindu. Ia menjadi penjaga arah utara dan pelindung dunia.",
      "en": "Kubera is the Hindu god of wealth and king of yakshas. He is regent of the north and protector of the world.",

      "id": "Penggambaran umum memperlihatkan tubuh gemuk, perhiasan, wadah uang, dan gada. Dalam kisah kitab, Ravana menggulingkannya dari Lanka sebelum ia menetap di Alaka di Himalaya.       "source_id": "kubera-s1",
      "quote": "is the god of wealth, and the god-king of the semi-divine yakshas in Hinduism.",

    {
      "id": "kubera-c02",
      "source_id": "kubera-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "id": "kubera-c03",
      "source_id": "kubera-s1",
      "quote": "Kubera is often depicted with a plump body, adorned with jewels, and carrying a money-pot and a club.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran umum memperlihatkan tubuh gemuk, perhiasan, wadah uang, dan gada.",
        "en": "Common depictions show a plump body, jewels, money pot, and club."
      }

      "id": "kubera-c04",
      "source_id": "kubera-s1",
      "quote": "Originally described as the chief of evil spirits in Vedic-era texts, Kubera acquired the status of a deva (god) only in the Puranas and the Hindu epics. The scriptures describe that Kubera once ruled Lanka, but was overthrown by his half-brother Ravana, later settling in the city of Alaka in the Himalayas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah kitab, Ravana menggulingkannya dari Lanka sebelum ia menetap di Alaka di Himalaya.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kubera-c05",
      "source_id": "kubera-s1",
      "quote": "In Buddhism, he is known as Vaisravana, the patronymic used of the Hindu Kubera and is also equated with Pañcika, while in Jainism, he is known as Sarvanubhuti. In Indonesia, Kubera is also known as Daneswara.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Buddhisnya Vaisravana; di Indonesia ia juga dikenal sebagai Daneswara.",

      "id": "kubera-c06",
      "source_id": "kubera-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Chandra adalah dewa Bulan dalam Hindu yang terkait malam dan tumbuhan.",
    "en": "Chandra is the Hindu Moon god associated with night and plants.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Chandra adalah dewa Bulan dalam Hindu yang terkait malam dan tumbuhan. Ia termasuk Navagraha, sembilan planet dalam tradisi Hindu.",
      "en": "Chandra is the Hindu Moon god associated with night and plants. He belongs to the Navagraha, nine planets in Hindu tradition.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chandra-c01",
        "chandra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Chandra berarti terang atau berkilau dalam bahasa Sanskerta. Epitetnya mencakup Shashank, yang berarti bertanda kelinci. Waktu pemakaian Soma sebagai nama Bulan diperdebatkan oleh para sarjana. Mackenzie mengidentifikasi dewa Bulan Chandra dengan Soma.",
      "en": "Chandra means bright or shining in Sanskrit. His epithets include Shashank, meaning hare marked. The time when Soma became a name of the Moon is debated by scholars. Mackenzie identifies the Moon god Chandra with Soma.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "chandra-c03",
        "chandra-c04",
        "chandra-c05",
        "chandra-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Penyamaan Soma dengan Bulan memiliki perbedaan penanggalan menurut kajian yang dirangkum.",
    "en": "The identification of Soma with the Moon has differing datings in the summarized studies.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "chandra-c05"

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "chandra-c01",
      "source_id": "chandra-s1",
      "quote": "Chandra (Sanskrit: चन्द्र, romanized: Chandra, lit. 'shining' or 'moon'), also known as Soma (Sanskrit: सोम), is the Hindu god of the Moon, and is associated with the night, plants and vegetation. He is one of the Navagraha (nine planets of Hinduism).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chandra adalah dewa Bulan dalam Hindu yang terkait malam dan tumbuhan.",
        "en": "Chandra is the Hindu Moon god associated with night and plants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chandra-c02",
      "source_id": "chandra-s1",
      "quote": "Chandra (Sanskrit: चन्द्र, romanized: Chandra, lit. 'shining' or 'moon'), also known as Soma (Sanskrit: सोम), is the Hindu god of the Moon, and is associated with the night, plants and vegetation. He is one of the Navagraha (nine planets of Hinduism).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia termasuk Navagraha, sembilan planet dalam tradisi Hindu.",
        "en": "He belongs to the Navagraha, nine planets in Hindu tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chandra-c03",
      "source_id": "chandra-s1",
      "quote": "The word \"Chandra\" literally means \"bright, shining or glittering\" and is used for the \"Moon\" in Sanskrit and other Indo-Aryan languages. It is also the name of various other figures in Hindu mythology, including an asura and a Suryavamsha king. It is also a common Indian name and surname.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Chandra berarti terang atau berkilau dalam bahasa Sanskerta.",
        "en": "Chandra means bright or shining in Sanskrit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chandra-c04",
      "source_id": "chandra-s1",
      "quote": "Some of the epithets of Chandra include Soma (distill), Indu (bright drop), Shashank (hare-marked), Atrisuta (son of Atri), Shashin or Shachin (the essence), Taradhipa (lord of stars), Nishakara (the night maker), Nakshatrapati (lord of the Nakshatra), Oshadhipati (lord of herbs), Uduraj or Udupati (water lord), Kumudanatha (lord of lotuses), and Udupa (boat).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Epitetnya mencakup Shashank, yang berarti bertanda kelinci.",
        "en": "His epithets include Shashank, meaning hare marked."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chandra-c05",
      "source_id": "chandra-s1",
      "quote": "Soma is one of the most common other names used for the deity; but the earliest use of the word to refer to the Moon is a subject of scholarly debate. Some scholars state that the word Soma is occasionally used for the Moon in the Vedas, while other scholars suggest that such usage emerged only in the post-Vedic literature.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Waktu pemakaian Soma sebagai nama Bulan diperdebatkan oleh para sarjana.",
        "en": "The time when Soma became a name of the Moon is debated by scholars."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "chandra-c06",
      "source_id": "chandra-s2",
      "quote": "The moon is the god Chandra, who became identified\r\nwith Soma.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 268",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie mengidentifikasi dewa Bulan Chandra dengan Soma.",
        "en": "Mackenzie identifies the Moon god Chandra with Soma."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Ganga personifies the Ganges and is worshipped as goddess of purification and forgiveness.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ganga adalah personifikasi Sungai Gangga yang dipuja sebagai dewi pemurnian dan pengampunan. Ia lazim digambarkan mengendarai makara, makhluk ilahi yang menyerupai buaya.",
      "en": "Ganga personifies the Ganges and is worshipped as goddess of purification and forgiveness. She is commonly depicted riding a makara, a divine crocodile like creature.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ganga-c01",
        "ganga-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagian kitab menyebutnya putri Himavat dan kakak Parvati, sedangkan versi lain mengaitkan asalnya dengan kaki Vishnu. Kisah turunnya ke bumi menuturkan Shiva menahan jatuhnya dengan rambutnya. Peziarah Hindu menenggelamkan abu keluarga di Gangga dalam keyakinan pemurnian menuju moksha. Dalam retelling Mackenzie, Ganga menjadi istri Shantanu dan menjanjikan kelahiran manusia bagi delapan Vasu yang terkena kutukan.",
      "en": "Some scriptures call her Himavat's daughter and Parvati's elder sister, while other versions connect her origin to Vishnu's feet. Her descent narrative recounts Shiva breaking her fall with his hair. Hindu pilgrims immerse relatives' ashes in the Ganges in the belief in purification toward moksha. In Mackenzie's retelling, Ganga becomes Shantanu's wife and promises human birth for eight cursed Vasus.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ganga-c01",
      "source_id": "ganga-s1",
      "quote": "Ganga (Sanskrit: गङ्गा, IAST: Gaṅgā) is the personification of the river Ganges, who is worshipped by Hindus as the goddess of purification and forgiveness. She is often depicted as a fair, beautiful ornate woman, riding a divine crocodile-like creature called the makara.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ganga adalah personifikasi Sungai Gangga yang dipuja sebagai dewi pemurnian dan pengampunan.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ganga-c02",
      "source_id": "ganga-s1",
      "quote": "Ganga (Sanskrit: गङ्गा, IAST: Gaṅgā) is the personification of the river Ganges, who is worshipped by Hindus as the goddess of purification and forgiveness. She is often depicted as a fair, beautiful ornate woman, riding a divine crocodile-like creature called the makara.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia lazim digambarkan mengendarai makara, makhluk ilahi yang menyerupai buaya.",
        "en": "She is commonly depicted riding a makara, a divine crocodile like creature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ganga-c03",
      "source_id": "ganga-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu mythology, Ganga is generally described as originating from the feet of the preserver deity Vishnu. However, according to some scriptures, including the Ramayana, she is the firstborn daughter of Himavat (the personification of the Himalayas) and the elder sister of the goddess Parvati.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian kitab menyebutnya putri Himavat dan kakak Parvati, sedangkan versi lain mengaitkan asalnya dengan kaki Vishnu.",
        "en": "Some scriptures call her Himavat's daughter and Parvati's elder sister, while other versions connect her origin to Vishnu's feet."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ganga-c04",
      "source_id": "ganga-s1",
      "quote": "This descent was facilitated by the god Shiva, who caught Ganga in his matted hair to break her fall and later released her gently onto the earth.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah turunnya ke bumi menuturkan Shiva menahan jatuhnya dengan rambutnya.",
        "en": "Her descent narrative recounts Shiva breaking her fall with his hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ganga-c05",
      "source_id": "ganga-s1",
      "quote": "Pilgrims immerse the ashes of their kin in the river Ganga, which is considered by them to bring the souls (purified spirits) closer to moksha, the liberation from the cycle of life and death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peziarah Hindu menenggelamkan abu keluarga di Gangga dalam keyakinan pemurnian menuju moksha.",
        "en": "Hindu pilgrims immerse relatives' ashes in the Ganges in the belief in purification toward moksha."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ganga-c06",
      "source_id": "ganga-s2",
      "quote": "His first wife was the goddess\r\nGanga of the Ganges river, and she was divinely\r\nbeautiful like to her kind. ... So they hastened to Ganga, and\r\nshe consented to become their human mother, promising\r\nthat she would cast them one by one into the Ganges\r\nsoon after birth, so that they might return speedily to\r\ntheir celestial state.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 898",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam retelling Mackenzie, Ganga menjadi istri Shantanu dan menjanjikan kelahiran manusia bagi delapan Vasu yang terkena kutukan.",
        "en": "In Mackenzie's retelling, Ganga becomes Shantanu's wife and promises human birth for eight cursed Vasus."

      "quote": "The first two epithets associate him with hala (langala, \"plough\") from his strong associations with farming and farmers, as the deity who used farm equipment as weapons when needed, and the next two refer to his strength.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Epitet Haladhara dan Halayudha berhubungan dengan bajak dan pertanian.",
        "en": "The epithets Haladhara and Halayudha connect him with the plough and farming."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "balarama-c05",
      "source_id": "balarama-s1",
      "quote": "Originally an agricultural deity, Balarama is mostly described as an incarnation of Shesha, the serpent associated with the deity Vishnu while some Vaishnava traditions regard him as the eighth avatar of Vishnu, with Jayadeva’s Gita Govinda (c.1200) \"incorporat[ing] Balarama into the pantheon\" as the ninth of the 10 principal avatars of Vishnu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia biasanya dipandang sebagai inkarnasi Shesha, tetapi sebagian tradisi menempatkannya sebagai avatar Vishnu.",
        "en": "He is usually regarded as an incarnation of Shesha, but some traditions count him as an avatar of Vishnu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "balarama-c06",
      "source_id": "balarama-s2",
      "quote": "Balarama is an incarnation of the world serpent\r\nShesha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 741",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menjelaskan Balarama sebagai inkarnasi ular dunia Shesha.",
        "en": "Mackenzie describes Balarama as an incarnation of the world serpent Shesha."
      }
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "rudra-c03",
      "source_id": "rudra-s1",
      "quote": "The etymology of the theonym Rudra is uncertain. It is usually derived from the Proto-Indo-European (PIE) root rud- (related to English rude), which means 'to cry, howl'. The name Rudra may thus be translated as 'the roarer'.",

      }

    {
      "id": "rudra-c04",
      "source_id": "rudra-s1",
      "quote": "The adjective śiva (shiva) in the sense of 'propitious' or 'kind' is first applied to the Rudra in RV 10.92.9.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The word shiva meaning kind or propitious is applied to Rudra in Rigveda 10.92.9."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rudra-c05",
      "source_id": "rudra-s1",
      "quote": "Rudra is called 'the archer' (Sanskrit: Śarva) and the arrow is an essential attribute of Rudra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Sharva dan atribut panah berkaitan dengan penggambarannya sebagai pemanah.",
        "en": "The name Sharva and the arrow attribute connect with his depiction as archer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "rudra-c06",
      "source_id": "rudra-s2",
      "quote": "Another wind or storm god is Rudra, also the father\r\nof the Maruts, who are called “Rudras”.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 232",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Mackenzie describes Rudra as a wind or storm god and father of the Maruts."
      }

      "en": "Karna is a major character in the Hindu epic Mahabharata and son of Surya and Kunti. Radha and Adhiratha find and foster him after he is abandoned in a basket on the Ganges.",

      "source_id": "karna-s1",
      "quote": "is one of the major characters in the Hindu epic Mahābhārata. He is the son of Surya (the solar deity) and princess Kunti (later the Pandava mother).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karna adalah tokoh utama wiracarita Hindu Mahabharata dan putra Surya serta Kunti.",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karna-c02",
      "source_id": "karna-s1",
      "quote": "she is forced to abandon the infant in a basket on the Ganges. He is discovered and fostered by Radha and Adhiratha Nandana, the sūta (charioteer) of King Dhritarashtra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",

      "id": "karna-c03",
      "source_id": "karna-s1",
      "quote": "He becomes a loyal friend of Duryodhana, and is appointed the king of Anga (Bihar-Bengal) by the prince.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi sahabat Duryodhana dan diangkat sebagai raja Anga.",
        "en": "He becomes Duryodhana's friend and is appointed king of Anga."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karna-c04",
      "source_id": "karna-s1",
      "quote": "Karna joins the Kaurava side of the Kurukshetra War, and is ultimately slain by Arjuna.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia berperang di pihak Kaurava dan akhirnya dibunuh Arjuna.",
        "en": "He fights on the Kaurava side and is ultimately killed by Arjuna."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karna-c05",
      "source_id": "karna-s1",
      "quote": "In the Mahabharata and the Puranas, it is the name of a warrior character. Called Vasusena as a child by his foster parents, he became known by the name Karna because of the golden earrings of Surya he used to wear, according to the Sanskrit epics scholar David Slavitt.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut penjelasan David Slavitt, nama masa kecilnya Vasusena dan nama Karna dikaitkan dengan anting emas Surya.",
        "en": "According to David Slavitt's explanation, his childhood name is Vasusena and the name Karna is linked to Surya's golden earrings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "karna-c06",
      "source_id": "karna-s2",
      "quote": "In Mahabharata there is a fragment of an old legend\r\nwhich relates the origin of Karna, the son of Queen\r\nPritha and the sun god: the birth of the child is concealed,\r\nand he is placed in a basket which is set afloat on\r\nthe river and is carried to a distant country.[72]",

    "en": "Mara in Siddhartha's awakening story appears as a powerful deity trying to obstruct his awakening.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "mara-papiyan-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Mara dalam kisah pencerahan Siddhartha tampil sebagai dewa kuat yang berupaya menggagalkan pencerahannya. Ia menggunakan pasukan surgawi dan penampakan perempuan cantik; beberapa legenda menyebut mereka putrinya.",
      "en": "Mara in Siddhartha's awakening story appears as a powerful deity trying to obstruct his awakening. He uses a celestial army and a vision of beautiful women; some legends call them his daughters.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "mara-papiyan-c01",
        "mara-papiyan-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kosmologi Buddhis, Mara dikaitkan dengan kematian, kelahiran kembali, dan hasrat. Epitet Papiyan menggambarkan sosok yang berkaitan dengan kejahatan, kesedihan, dan kemalangan. Nama Mara berasal dari akar Sanskerta yang berkaitan dengan menyebabkan kematian atau membunuh. Kajian Guruge mengutip definisi Mara sebagai personifikasi kematian, kejahatan, dan godaan.",
      "en": "In Buddhist cosmology Mara is associated with death, rebirth, and desire. The epithet Papiyan describes a being associated with evil, sorrow, and misfortune. Mara's name derives from a Sanskrit root associated with causing death or killing. Guruge's study quotes a definition of Mara as personification of Death, the Evil One, and the Tempter.",

        "mara-papiyan-c05",
        "mara-papiyan-c06"

      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "mara-papiyan-c01",
      "source_id": "mara-papiyan-s1",
      "quote": "In the story of the Awakening of Prince Siddhartha Māra appears as a powerful deva, trying to seduce him with his celestial army and a vision of beautiful maidens (accharā) who, in various legends, are often said to be Mara's daughters. In other iterations, Māra is inspired by Hindu myths about asuras, a class of titans or lesser deities.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mara dalam kisah pencerahan Siddhartha tampil sebagai dewa kuat yang berupaya menggagalkan pencerahannya.",
        "en": "Mara in Siddhartha's awakening story appears as a powerful deity trying to obstruct his awakening."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mara-papiyan-c02",
      "source_id": "mara-papiyan-s1",
      "quote": "In the story of the Awakening of Prince Siddhartha Māra appears as a powerful deva, trying to seduce him with his celestial army and a vision of beautiful maidens (accharā) who, in various legends, are often said to be Mara's daughters. In other iterations, Māra is inspired by Hindu myths about asuras, a class of titans or lesser deities.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menggunakan pasukan surgawi dan penampakan perempuan cantik; beberapa legenda menyebut mereka putrinya.",
        "en": "He uses a celestial army and a vision of beautiful women; some legends call them his daughters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mara-papiyan-c03",
      "source_id": "mara-papiyan-s1",
      "quote": "In Buddhist cosmology, Mara is associated with death, rebirth and desire. Nyanaponika Thera has described Mara as \"the personification of the forces antagonistic to enlightenment.\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kosmologi Buddhis, Mara dikaitkan dengan kematian, kelahiran kembali, dan hasrat.",
        "en": "In Buddhist cosmology Mara is associated with death, rebirth, and desire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mara-papiyan-c04",
      "source_id": "mara-papiyan-s1",
      "quote": "His name is first mentioned in the Atharva Veda (1200 BCE–1000 BCE) as  Mrtyu and Agha Mara, the evil slayer. He is called the \"evil one who kills\" and \"Papiyan\", denoting a being which is not only morally bad but intertwined with sorrow, pain and misfortune.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Epitet Papiyan menggambarkan sosok yang berkaitan dengan kejahatan, kesedihan, dan kemalangan.",
        "en": "The epithet Papiyan describes a being associated with evil, sorrow, and misfortune."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mara-papiyan-c05",
      "source_id": "mara-papiyan-s1",
      "quote": "The word Māra comes from the Sanskrit form of the verbal root mṛ. It takes a present indicative form mṛyate and a causative form mārayati (with strengthening of the root vowel from ṛ to ār). Māra is a verbal noun from the causative root and means 'causing death' or 'killing'. It is related to other words for death from the same root, such as: maraṇa and mṛtyu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Mara berasal dari akar Sanskerta yang berkaitan dengan menyebabkan kematian atau membunuh.",
        "en": "Mara's name derives from a Sanskrit root associated with causing death or killing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "mara-papiyan-c06",
      "source_id": "mara-papiyan-s2",
      "quote": "Maara as \"the personification of Death, the Evil One, the Tempter",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kajian Guruge mengutip definisi Mara sebagai personifikasi kematian, kejahatan, dan godaan.",
        "en": "Guruge's study quotes a definition of Mara as personification of Death, the Evil One, and the Tempter."
      }
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "nandi-hinduism-c01",
      "source_id": "nandi-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "is the bull vahana (mount) of the Hindu god Shiva. He is also the guardian deity of Kailash, the abode of Shiva.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nandi adalah banteng tunggangan Shiva sekaligus dewa penjaga Kailash.",
        "en": "Nandi is Shiva's bull mount and the guardian deity of Kailash."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nandi-hinduism-c02",
      "source_id": "nandi-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "Nandi (Sanskrit: नन्दि), also known as Nandikeshvara or Nandideva, is the bull vahana (mount) of the Hindu god Shiva.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama lainnya Nandikeshvara dan Nandideva.",
        "en": "His other names are Nandikeshvara and Nandideva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nandi-hinduism-c03",
      "source_id": "nandi-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "Almost all Shiva temples display stone images of a seated Nandi, generally facing the main shrine.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hampir semua kuil Shiva menampilkan patung Nandi duduk menghadap tempat suci utama.",
        "en": "Almost all Shiva temples display a seated Nandi image facing the main shrine."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nandi-hinduism-c04",
      "source_id": "nandi-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "The Sanskrit word nandi (Sanskrit: नन्दि) means happy, joy, and satisfaction, which are the properties of Nandi, the divine guardian of Shiva.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nandi dalam bahasa Sanskerta berarti bahagia, sukacita, atau kepuasan.",
        "en": "Nandi in Sanskrit means happy, joy, or satisfaction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nandi-hinduism-c05",
      "source_id": "nandi-hinduism-s1",
      "quote": "Nandi is described as the son of the sage Shilada. Shilada underwent severe penance to have a boon– a child with immortality and blessings of Shiva, and received Nandi as his son.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah menyebutnya putra resi Shilada yang menjalankan tapa untuk memperoleh anak.",
        "en": "The narrative calls him the son of sage Shilada who practised penance to obtain a child."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nandi-hinduism-c06",
      "source_id": "nandi-hinduism-s2",
      "quote": "The bull symbol, Nandi, the moon crescent on his\r\nforehead, and the serpent girdle, indicate that Shiva is a\r\ngod of fertility.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 814",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menyebut Nandi sebagai simbol banteng Shiva.",
        "en": "Mackenzie calls Nandi Shiva's bull symbol."
      }
    }

      "id": "Dalam Jainisme, tirthankara adalah penyelamat dan pengkhotbah dharma yang membimbing makhluk melewati samsara. Menurut keyakinan Jain, ia mencapai kevala jnana, pengetahuan sempurna.",
      "en": "In Jainism a tirthankara is a saviour and preacher of dharma guiding beings across samsara. In Jain belief he attains kevala jnana, omniscience.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tirthankara-c01",
        "tirthankara-c02"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Kosmologi Jain menyebut 24 tirthankara muncul dalam setiap setengah siklus waktu. Rishabhanatha adalah yang pertama dan Mahavira yang terakhir dalam siklus yang sekarang. Tirthankara mengorganisasi sangha yang terdiri dari empat kelompok pengikut laki-laki dan perempuan, monastik dan awam. Dalam keyakinan Jain, rahmat mereka tersedia bagi seluruh makhluk tanpa memandang agamanya.",
      "en": "Jain cosmology says 24 tirthankaras appear in each half cycle of time. Rishabhanatha is the first and Mahavira the last in the current cycle.       "id": "tirthankara-c01",
      "source_id": "tirthankara-s1",
      "quote": "In Jainism, a Tirthaṅkara (IAST: tīrthaṅkara; lit. 'ford-maker') is a saviour and supreme preacher of the dharma (righteous path). The term refers to one who establishes a tirtha, a fordable passage across saṃsāra, the sea of interminable birth and death.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Jainisme, tirthankara adalah penyelamat dan pengkhotbah dharma yang membimbing makhluk melewati samsara.",
        "en": "In Jainism a tirthankara is a saviour and preacher of dharma guiding beings across samsara."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tirthankara-c02",
      "source_id": "tirthankara-s1",
      "quote": "After understanding the true nature of the self or soul, the Tīrthaṅkara attains kevala jnana (omniscience).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut keyakinan Jain, ia mencapai kevala jnana, pengetahuan sempurna.",
        "en": "In Jain belief he attains kevala jnana, omniscience."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tirthankara-c03",
      "source_id": "tirthankara-s1",
      "quote": "In each half of the cycle, exactly 24 tirthaṅkaras are said to appear in this part of the universe.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kosmologi Jain menyebut 24 tirthankara muncul dalam setiap setengah siklus waktu.",
        "en": "Jain cosmology says 24 tirthankaras appear in each half cycle of time."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tirthankara-c04",
      "source_id": "tirthankara-s1",
      "quote": "The first tirthaṅkara in the present cycle (Hunda Avsarpinī) was Rishabhanatha, who is credited with formulating and organising humans to live in a society harmoniously. The 24th and last tirthaṅkara of the present half-cycle is traditionally identified as Mahavira (599 BCE–527 BCE).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rishabhanatha adalah yang pertama dan Mahavira yang terakhir dalam siklus yang sekarang.",
        "en": "Rishabhanatha is the first and Mahavira the last in the current cycle."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tirthankara-c05",
      "source_id": "tirthankara-s1",
      "quote": "A tirthaṅkara organises the sangha, a fourfold order of male and female monastics, śrāvakas (male followers) and śrāvikās (female followers).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tirthankara mengorganisasi sangha yang terdiri dari empat kelompok pengikut laki-laki dan perempuan, monastik dan awam.",
        "en": "A tirthankara organizes a fourfold sangha of male and female monastics and lay followers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tirthankara-c06",
      "source_id": "tirthankara-s1",
      "quote": "While Jains document and revere tirthaṅkaras, their grace is said to be available to all living beings regardless of religion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam keyakinan Jain, rahmat mereka tersedia bagi seluruh makhluk tanpa memandang agamanya.",

      "id": "Dalam tradisi itu Vairocana adalah Dharmakaya, tubuh Dharma semua Buddha. Dalam Vajrayana, tubuh Buddha kosmis Mahavairocana dipahami sebagai keseluruhan alam semesta. Ia merupakan tokoh pusat di antara Lima Buddha dari Lima Arah. HAR menjelaskan bahwa Tantra Abhisambodhi Vairocana tidak memakai sistem Lima Buddha seperti klasifikasi mandala Yoga dan Anuttarayoga.",
      "en": "In these traditions Vairocana is the Dharmakaya, Dharma body of all Buddhas. In Vajrayana the cosmic Buddha Mahavairocana's body is understood as the entire universe.     {
      "id": "vairocana-c03",
      "source_id": "vairocana-s1",
      "quote": "In Mahayana and Vajrayana Buddhism, Vairocana is the Dharmakāya (lit. 'Dharma-body') of all Buddhas (the Dharma-body is the \"true body\" of all Buddhas, equivalent to the Ultimate Reality), which is formless, omnipresent, self-existent, eternal, indestructible, unable to be defiled, and is the source of all manifestations.",

    {
      "id": "vairocana-c04",
      "source_id": "vairocana-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

    {
      "id": "vairocana-c05",
      "source_id": "vairocana-s1",
      "quote": "Among the Five Buddhas of the Five Directions, Vairocana is the central figure, symbolizing both the source and destination of all the other four.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      }

    {
      "id": "vairocana-c06",
      "source_id": "vairocana-s2",

      "source_id": "ardhanarisvara-s1",
      "quote": "Ardhanarishvara (Sanskrit: अर्धनारीश्वर, romanized: Ardhanārīśvara, lit. 'the half-female Lord') is a form of the Hindu deity Shiva combined with his consort Parvati. Ardhanarishvara is depicted as half-male and half-female, equally split down the middle.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      "id": "ardhanarisvara-c02",
      "source_id": "ardhanarisvara-s1",
      "quote": "Ardhanarishvara (Sanskrit: अर्धनारीश्वर, romanized: Ardhanārīśvara, lit. 'the half-female Lord') is a form of the Hindu deity Shiva combined with his consort Parvati. Ardhanarishvara is depicted as half-male and half-female, equally split down the middle.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The form is divided equally into half male and half female."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ardhanarisvara-c03",
      "source_id": "ardhanarisvara-s1",
      "quote": "Ardhanarishvara represents the synthesis of masculine and feminine energies of the universe (Purusha and Prakriti) and illustrates how Shakti, the female principle of God, is inseparable from (or the same as, according to some interpretations) Shiva, the male principle of God, and vice versa.       "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    },
    {

      "source_id": "ardhanarisvara-s1",
      "quote": "The right half is usually the male Shiva, illustrating his traditional attributes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagian kanan biasanya Shiva laki-laki.",
        "en": "The right half is usually male Shiva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ardhanarisvara-c05",
      "source_id": "ardhanarisvara-s1",
      "quote": "The earliest Ardhanarishvara images are dated to the Kushan period, starting from the first century CE. Its iconography evolved and was perfected in the Gupta era. The Puranas and various iconographic treatises write about the mythology and iconography of Ardhanarishvara.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Daksha adalah dewa Hindu yang dalam Rigveda merupakan Aditya terkait keterampilan kependetaan.",
    "en": "Daksha is a Hindu deity who in the Rigveda is an Aditya associated with priestly skills.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "daksha-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Daksha adalah dewa Hindu yang dalam Rigveda merupakan Aditya terkait keterampilan kependetaan. Dalam wiracarita dan Purana ia adalah putra Brahma dan salah satu Prajapati, agen penciptaan.",
      "en": "Daksha is a Hindu deity who in the Rigveda is an Aditya associated with priestly skills. In epics and Puranas he is Brahma's son and one of the Prajapatis, agents of creation.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "daksha-c01",
        "daksha-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisahnya menyebut pengorbanan yang sengaja tidak mengundang Sati dan Shiva. Dalam versi Linga Purana, Virabhadra memenggalnya dan ia kemudian hidup kembali berkepala kambing. Ikonografinya berupa laki-laki bertubuh kekar dengan wajah manusia atau kepala kambing. Dalam retelling Mackenzie, Shiva sendiri memenggal Daksha dan mengganti kepalanya dengan kepala kambing.",
      "en": "His narrative describes a sacrifice deliberately excluding Sati and Shiva. In the Linga Purana version Virabhadra beheads him and he later revives with a goat's head. His iconography is a stocky man with a human face or goat's head. In Mackenzie's retelling Shiva himself beheads Daksha and replaces his head with a goat's head.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "daksha-c03",
        "daksha-c04",
        "daksha-c05",
        "daksha-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "daksha-c01",
      "source_id": "daksha-s1",
      "quote": "is a Hindu god whose role underwent a significant transformation from Vedic to Itihasa-Puranic mythology. In the Rigveda, Daksha is an aditya and is associated with priestly skills.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Daksha adalah dewa Hindu yang dalam Rigveda merupakan Aditya terkait keterampilan kependetaan.",
        "en": "Daksha is a Hindu deity who in the Rigveda is an Aditya associated with priestly skills."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "daksha-c02",
      "source_id": "daksha-s1",
      "quote": "In the epics and Puranic scriptures, he is a son of the creator-god Brahma and one of the Prajapati, the agents of creation, as well as a divine king-rishi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam wiracarita dan Purana ia adalah putra Brahma dan salah satu Prajapati, agen penciptaan.",
        "en": "In epics and Puranas he is Brahma's son and one of the Prajapatis, agents of creation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "daksha-c03",
      "source_id": "daksha-s1",
      "quote": "a resentful Daksha conducted a yajna (fire-sacrifice), and deliberately did not invite his youngest daughter Sati and her husband Shiva.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya menyebut pengorbanan yang sengaja tidak mengundang Sati dan Shiva.",
        "en": "His narrative describes a sacrifice deliberately excluding Sati and Shiva."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "daksha-c04",
      "source_id": "daksha-s1",
      "quote": "In the Linga Purana, for insulting Shiva during this event, which caused Sati to self-immolate in fury, he was beheaded by Virabhadra, a fearsome form of Shiva. He was later resurrected with the head of a goat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi Linga Purana, Virabhadra memenggalnya dan ia kemudian hidup kembali berkepala kambing.",
        "en": "In the Linga Purana version Virabhadra beheads him and he later revives with a goat's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "daksha-c05",
      "source_id": "daksha-s1",
      "quote": "His iconography depicts him as a man with a stocky body and a handsome face or the head of a goat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikonografinya berupa laki-laki bertubuh kekar dengan wajah manusia atau kepala kambing.",
        "en": "His iconography is a stocky man with a human face or goat's head."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "daksha-c06",
      "source_id": "daksha-s2",
      "quote": "Daksha is the father of Sati, the peerless wife of\r\nShiva. It was on account of this rishi's quarrel with\r\nher husband, who was not invited to a great feast, that\r\nshe flung herself upon the sacrificial fire. Shiva cut off\r\nDaksha's head and replaced it with the head of a goat.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 839",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam retelling Mackenzie, Shiva sendiri memenggal Daksha dan mengganti kepalanya dengan kepala kambing.",
        "en": "In Mackenzie's retelling Shiva himself beheads Daksha and replaces his head with a goat's head."
      }
    }
  ],

    {

      "source_id": "tara-s1",
      "quote": "Tārā is also known as a saviouress who hears the cries of beings in saṃsāra and saves them from worldly and spiritual danger.",

    {
      "id": "tara-c03",
      "source_id": "tara-s1",
      "quote": "Both Green and White Tārā remain popular meditation deities or yidams in Tibetan Buddhism, and Tara is also revered in Newar Buddhism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tara-c04",
      "source_id": "tara-s1",
      "quote": "Green Tara emanates twenty-one Tārās, each with different attributes—colors, implements, and activities such as pacifying (śānti), increasing (pauṣṭika), enthralling (vaśīkaraṇa), and wrathful (abhicāra).",

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Green Tara emanates twenty one Taras with different attributes and activities."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tara-c05",
      "source_id": "tara-s1",
      "quote": "Tārā (Devanagari: तारा) is a feminine noun derived from the root √tṝ, \"to cross\". It is causative, and as such means \"to cause to cross\", i.e., \"to rescue\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Tara dihubungkan dengan akar Sanskerta untuk menyeberang, dalam makna menyebabkan menyeberang atau menyelamatkan.",
        "en": "Tara's name is linked to a Sanskrit root for crossing, in the sense of causing to cross or rescuing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tara-c06",
      "source_id": "tara-s2",
      "quote": "Practiced in all Schools of Tibetan Buddhism Tara is second in popularity only to Avalokiteshvara.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "HAR menyatakan Tara dipraktikkan dalam semua aliran Buddhisme Tibet.",

      "id": "Ia disebut Dandapani karena membawa tongkat untuk menghukum pelaku dosa. Epitet Shvashva berarti ia memiliki kendaraan anjing. Kisah Shiva Purana menuturkan Bhairava memenggal kepala kelima Brahma untuk menghukum kebohongannya. Dalam lanjutan kisah, tengkorak Brahma terlepas dari tangannya setelah ia tiba di Kashi.",

      "source_id": "bhairava-s1",
      "quote": "Bhairava is also called Daṇḍapāni (\"he who holds the daṇḍa in his hand\"), as he holds a rod or danda to punish sinners,",

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bhairava-c04",
      "source_id": "bhairava-s1",
      "quote": "Śvāśva, meaning, \"he whose vehicle is a dog\". In Vajrayana Buddhism, he is considered a fierce emanation",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

    {
      "id": "bhairava-c05",
      "source_id": "bhairava-s1",
      "quote": "Bhairava decapitated the fifth head of Brahma for uttering the falsehood.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Shiva Purana menuturkan Bhairava memenggal kepala kelima Brahma untuk menghukum kebohongannya.",
        "en": "The Shiva Purana narrative recounts Bhairava beheading Brahma's fifth head to punish his lie."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bhairava-c06",
      "source_id": "bhairava-s1",
      "quote": "When Bhairava visited Kashi, the city of the liberated, Brahmahatya left him, and the skull of Brahma was released from his hand.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam lanjutan kisah, tengkorak Brahma terlepas dari tangannya setelah ia tiba di Kashi.",

      "en": "Jagannath is a Hindu deity worshipped with his siblings Balabhadra and Subhadra. Most Vaishnavas regard him as a regional form of Krishna or Vishnu.",

Nama Jagannath menggabungkan jagat dan natha dalam arti penguasa alam semesta. Mackenzie menuturkan ikon Jagannath dibawa dalam kereta besar yang ditarik pemuja sekali setahun.",

  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "jagannath-c01",
      "source_id": "jagannath-s1",
      "quote": "Jagannath (Sanskrit: जगन्नाथ, Odia: ଜଗନ୍ନାଥ, romanized: Jagannātha, lit. 'Lord of the Universe', Odia pronunciation: [dʒɔɡɔnːat̪ʰɔ]; formerly English: Juggernaut) is a Hindu deity worshipped as part of a triad along with his brother, Balabhadra, and his sister, Subhadra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jagannath adalah dewa Hindu yang dipuja bersama saudaranya Balabhadra dan Subhadra.",
        "en": "Jagannath is a Hindu deity worshipped with his siblings Balabhadra and Subhadra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jagannath-c02",
      "source_id": "jagannath-s1",
      "quote": "Jagannath, within Odia Hinduism, is the supreme god, Purushottama, and the Para Brahman. To most Vaishnava Hindus, Jagannath is a regional form of Krishna, sometimes as a form of Vishnu. To some Shaiva and Shakta Hindus, he is a symmetry-filled tantric form of Bhairava, a fierce manifestation of Shiva associated with annihilation.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian besar Waisnawa memandangnya sebagai bentuk regional Krishna atau Vishnu.",
        "en": "Most Vaishnavas regard him as a regional form of Krishna or Vishnu."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jagannath-c03",
      "source_id": "jagannath-s1",
      "quote": "The idol of Jagannath is a carved and decorated wooden stump with large round eyes and a symmetric face, and the idol has a conspicuous absence of hands or legs.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikonnya berupa kayu berhias dengan mata bulat besar dan tanpa tangan atau kaki yang menonjol.",
        "en": "His icon is decorated wood with large round eyes and no conspicuous hands or legs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jagannath-c04",
      "source_id": "jagannath-s1",
      "quote": "Unusually, the icon is made of wood and replaced with a new one at regular intervals.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikon kayunya diganti secara berkala.",
        "en": "His wooden icon is replaced at regular intervals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "jagannath-c05",
      "source_id": "jagannath-s1",
      "quote": "Jagannath is a Sanskrit word, compounded of jagat meaning \"universe\" and nātha meaning \"Master\" or \"Lord\". Thus, Jagannath means \"lord of the universe\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Jagannath menggabungkan jagat dan natha dalam arti penguasa alam semesta.",
        "en": "The name Jagannath combines jagat and natha meaning Lord of the Universe."
      }

    {
      "id": "jagannath-c06",
      "source_id": "jagannath-s2",
      "quote": "Once a\r\nyear the idol is bathed and afterwards taken forth in a\r\ngreat car, which is dragged by pious worshippers.",

      "en": "Prithvi is the goddess personifying earth in Hinduism and a mother goddess in Vedic religion. In the Vedas she is described as fertile, generous, and sustaining all beings.",

      "quote": "is the Sanskrit name for the Earth, as well as the name of the goddess-personification of it in Hinduism. The goddess Prithvi is an archetypal Mother Goddess, and one of the most important goddesses in the historical Vedic religion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Prithvi adalah dewi personifikasi bumi dalam Hindu dan merupakan dewi ibu dalam agama Weda.",
        "en": "Prithvi is the goddess personifying earth in Hinduism and a mother goddess in Vedic religion."

      "source_id": "prithvi-s1",

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Weda ia digambarkan subur, murah hati, dan memberi makan semua makhluk.",

      "source_id": "prithvi-s1",

She is frequently addressed as a mother, and a nurturing, generous goddess who provides sustenance to all beings living on her vast, firm expanse. While the Rigveda predominantly associates her with Dyaus ('Father Sky'), the Atharvaveda and later texts portray her as an independent deity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda mengaitkannya dengan Dyaus, Bapa Langit; Atharvaveda menampilkannya sebagai dewi mandiri.",
        "en": "The Rigveda associates her with Dyaus, Father Sky; the Atharvaveda presents her as an independent goddess."
      }

    {
      "id": "prithvi-c04",
      "source_id": "prithvi-s1",
      "quote": "In classical Hinduism, the figure of Prithvi is supplanted by the goddess Bhumi, while the term Prithvi serves as one of her epithets. She becomes significantly associated with Vishnu, one of the most important gods in later Hinduism, and two of his dashavatars—Varaha and Prithu.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",

      }

      "id": "prithvi-c05",
      "source_id": "prithvi-s1",
      "quote": "Another connection is found between Prithvi and the mythological figure Prithu (Sanskrit: पृथु, Pṛthu), who chases the goddess Prthvi, shapeshifted as a cow.       "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Prithu menuturkan dewi bumi berubah menjadi sapi.",
        "en": "The Prithu narrative recounts the earth goddess changing into a cow."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "prithvi-c06",
      "source_id": "prithvi-s2",
      "quote": "THOU, of a truth, O Prthivi, bearest the tool that rends the hills: ... Thou rich in torrents, who with might quickenest earth, O Mighty One.",

      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Rigveda 5.84 invokes Prithvi bearing the tool that rends hills and quickening earth."

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sani adalah personifikasi ilahi planet Saturnus dan anggota Navagraha dalam Hindu. Penggambarannya mencakup tubuh gelap dengan pedang atau tongkat serta tunggangan kerbau atau gagak.",
      "en": "Shani is the divine personification of Saturn and a member of the Navagraha in Hinduism. Depictions include a dark figure with sword or sceptre and buffalo or crow mount.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sani-c01",
        "sani-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia merupakan dewa karma, waktu, dan balasan atas pikiran, ucapan, serta tindakan. Dalam kisah, istri Sani mengutuk tatapannya agar menyebabkan kehancuran atau kesulitan. Berbagai teks berbeda menggambarkan tunggangannya sebagai gagak, burung nasar, kereta, atau hewan lain. Mackenzie menuturkan versi mitos bahwa Saturnus yang terkutuk memenggal Ganesha dengan tatapannya.",
      "en": "He is god of karma, time, and retribution for thoughts, speech, and deeds. In the narrative Shani's wife curses his gaze to cause destruction or hardship. Different texts depict his mount as crow, vulture, chariot, or other animals. Mackenzie recounts a myth in which cursed Saturn decapitates Ganesha through his gaze.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sani-c03",
        "sani-c04",
        "sani-c05",
        "sani-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,

  "story_mode": null,

      "id": "sani-c01",
      "source_id": "sani-s1",
      "quote": "is the divine personification of the planet Saturn in Hinduism, and is one of the nine heavenly objects (Navagraha) in Hindu astrology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sani adalah personifikasi ilahi planet Saturnus dan anggota Navagraha dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Shani is the divine personification of Saturn and a member of the Navagraha in Hinduism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sani-c02",
      "source_id": "sani-s1",
      "quote": "whose iconography consists of a figure with a dark complexion carrying a sword or danda (sceptre) and sitting on a buffalo or some times on a crow.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambarannya mencakup tubuh gelap dengan pedang atau tongkat serta tunggangan kerbau atau gagak.",
        "en": "Depictions include a dark figure with sword or sceptre and buffalo or crow mount."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sani-c03",
      "source_id": "sani-s1",
      "quote": "He is the god of karma, time and retribution, and delivers results depending upon one's thoughts, speech, and deeds.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia merupakan dewa karma, waktu, dan balasan atas pikiran, ucapan, serta tindakan.",
        "en": "He is god of karma, time, and retribution for thoughts, speech, and deeds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sani-c04",
      "source_id": "sani-s1",
      "quote": "Shani is married to the daughter of gandharva Chitraratha, who cursed him that anyone he gazed upon would face destruction or hardship.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kisah, istri Sani mengutuk tatapannya agar menyebabkan kehancuran atau kesulitan.",
        "en": "In the narrative Shani's wife curses his gaze to cause destruction or hardship."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sani-c05",
      "source_id": "sani-s1",
      "quote": "Shani is depicted wearing blue or black robes, having a dark complexion and riding a vulture or on an iron chariot drawn by eight horses. He holds in his hands a bow, an arrow, an axe and a trident. He is canonically represented riding on a large crow or vulture which follows him wherever he goes.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbagai teks berbeda menggambarkan tunggangannya sebagai gagak, burung nasar, kereta, atau hewan lain.",
        "en": "Different texts depict his mount as crow, vulture, chariot, or other animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sani-c06",
      "source_id": "sani-s2",
      "quote": "A myth in one of the\r\nPuranas relates that the planet Saturn, being under a\r\ncurse, decapitated Ganesa simply by looking at him.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 829",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan versi mitos bahwa Saturnus yang terkutuk memenggal Ganesha dengan tatapannya.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts a myth in which cursed Saturn decapitates Ganesha through his gaze."
      }
    }
  ],
