      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Airavata adalah gajah ilahi putih yang menjadi raja gajah dan tunggangan Indra.",
        "en": "Airavata is a white divine elephant, king of elephants, and Indra's mount."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airavata-c02",
      "source_id": "airavata-s1",
      "quote": "He is also called 'Abhra-matanga', meaning \"elephant of the clouds\"",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Epitet Abhra-matanga berarti gajah awan.",
        "en": "The epithet Abhra matanga means elephant of the clouds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airavata-c03",
      "source_id": "airavata-s1",
      "quote": "'Abhramu' is the elephant wife of Airavata.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Abhramu disebut sebagai pasangan gajah Airavata.",
        "en": "Abhramu is called Airavata's elephant consort."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airavata-c04",
      "source_id": "airavata-s1",
      "quote": "The appearance or birth of Airavata varies according to different Hindu texts. In the Ramayana, Airavata was born to Iravati (descendant of the sage, Kashyapa), whereas the Vishnu Purana notes that Airavata was born from the churning of the ocean of milk.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ramayana menyebutnya lahir dari Iravati, sementara Vishnu Purana menyebut kelahiran dari pengadukan samudra susu.",
        "en": "The Ramayana gives his birth from Iravati, while the Vishnu Purana gives birth from churning the milk ocean."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airavata-c05",
      "source_id": "airavata-s1",
      "quote": "Prithu made Airavata king of all elephants. The name of his consort, Abhramu, means \"the one who knits or binds the clouds,\" reflecting the idea that elephants can produce rain clouds. The connection of elephants with water and rain is emphasized in the mythology of Indra, who rides the elephant Airavata when he defeats Vritra.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda menghubungkan Airavata dan gajah dengan air serta hujan.",
        "en": "Legend connects Airavata and elephants with water and rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "airavata-c06",
      "source_id": "airavata-s2",
      "quote": "seated\r\nhimself upon a great white elephant, “the handsome and\r\never victorious”, named Airavata; it “was furnished with\r\nfour tusks”",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 178",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi Mackenzie menyebut Airavata sebagai gajah putih Indra bertaring empat.",
        "en": "Mackenzie's version calls Airavata Indra's white elephant with four tusks."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Dakini adalah jenis dewi dalam Hindu dan Buddha, dengan arti yang berbeda menurut tradisi.",
    "en": "A dakini is a type of goddess in Hinduism and Buddhism, with meanings varying by tradition.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dakini-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dakini adalah jenis dewi dalam Hindu dan Buddha, dengan arti yang berbeda menurut tradisi. Teks Hindu awal dan Buddhisme esoterik Asia Timur menggambarkan dakini sebagai kelompok perempuan demonis pemakan daging manusia.",
      "en": "A dakini is a type of goddess in Hinduism and Buddhism, with meanings varying by tradition. Early Hindu texts and East Asian esoteric Buddhism describe dakinis as a race of demonesses eating human flesh.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dakini-c01",
        "dakini-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam literatur Tantra Hindu, Dakini adalah nama dewi yang terkait cakra atau unsur tubuh. Dalam Buddha Nepal dan Tibet, dakini kebijaksanaan dipandang sebagai perwujudan perempuan dari energi tercerahkan. Di Jepang para dakini bergabung sebagai Dakiniten dan dikaitkan dengan ikonografi rubah melalui sinkretisme Inari. HAR menjelaskan dakini dalam Nyingma sebagai dewi perempuan atau makhluk tercerahkan.",
      "en": "In Hindu Tantric literature Dakini names a goddess associated with chakras or bodily elements. In Nepalese and Tibetan Buddhism wisdom dakinis are regarded as female embodiments of enlightened energy. In Japan the dakinis coalesce as Dakiniten and become associated with fox iconography through Inari syncretism. HAR explains dakini in Nyingma as a female deity or enlightened being.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dakini-c03",
        "dakini-c04",
        "dakini-c05",
        "dakini-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": {
    "id": "Istilah dakini berubah antara demon perempuan, dewi Tantra, dan perwujudan kebijaksanaan; konteks tradisinya menentukan makna.",
    "en": "Dakini varies between demonesses, Tantric goddesses, and embodiments of wisdom; the tradition determines the meaning.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "dakini-c02",
      "dakini-c03",
      "dakini-c04",

    ]
  },

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "dakini-c01",
      "source_id": "dakini-s1",
      "quote": "A dakini is a type of goddess in Hinduism and Buddhism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dakini adalah jenis dewi dalam Hindu dan Buddha, dengan arti yang berbeda menurut tradisi.",
        "en": "A dakini is a type of goddess in Hinduism and Buddhism, with meanings varying by tradition."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dakini-c02",
      "source_id": "dakini-s1",
      "quote": "in earlier Hindu texts and East Asian esoteric Buddhism, the term denotes a race of demonesses who ate the flesh and vital essence of humans.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Hindu awal dan Buddhisme esoterik Asia Timur menggambarkan dakini sebagai kelompok perempuan demonis pemakan daging manusia.",
        "en": "Early Hindu texts and East Asian esoteric Buddhism describe dakinis as a race of demonesses eating human flesh."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dakini-c03",
      "source_id": "dakini-s1",
      "quote": "In Hindu Tantric literature, Ḍākinī is the name of a goddess often associated with one of the six chakras or the seven fundamental elements (dhātu) of the human body.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam literatur Tantra Hindu, Dakini adalah nama dewi yang terkait cakra atau unsur tubuh.",
        "en": "In Hindu Tantric literature Dakini names a goddess associated with chakras or bodily elements."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dakini-c04",
      "source_id": "dakini-s1",
      "quote": "Meanwhile, in Nepalese and Tibetan Buddhism, dakini (or wisdom dakini) can refer to both what can be best described as fierce-looking female embodiments of enlightened energy,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Buddha Nepal dan Tibet, dakini kebijaksanaan dipandang sebagai perwujudan perempuan dari energi tercerahkan.",
        "en": "In Nepalese and Tibetan Buddhism wisdom dakinis are regarded as female embodiments of enlightened energy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dakini-c05",
      "source_id": "dakini-s1",
      "quote": "In Japan, the ḍākinīs – held in the East Asian Buddhist tradition to have been subjugated and converted to Buddhism by the buddha Vairocana under the guise of the god Mahākāla (Daikokuten in Japanese) – were eventually coalesced into a single deity called Dakiniten, who, after becoming syncretized with the native agricultural deity Inari, became linked to the fox (kitsune) iconography as",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Jepang para dakini bergabung sebagai Dakiniten dan dikaitkan dengan ikonografi rubah melalui sinkretisme Inari.",
        "en": "In Japan the dakinis coalesce as Dakiniten and become associated with fox iconography through Inari syncretism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dakini-c06",
      "source_id": "dakini-s2",
      "quote": "In the Nyingma Tradition of Tibetan Buddhism, a Dakini is a term generally used to describe a female deity or enlightened being.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "HAR menjelaskan dakini dalam Nyingma sebagai dewi perempuan atau makhluk tercerahkan.",
        "en": "HAR explains dakini in Nyingma as a female deity or enlightened being."
      }
    }

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Vishvakarma adalah dewa perajin dan arsitek ilahi para deva dalam Hindu. Nama itu semula merupakan epitet dewa kuat, kemudian menjadi nama dewa perajin.",
      "en": "Vishvakarma is a craftsman deity and divine architect of the devas in Hinduism. The name was originally an epithet for powerful deities and later became the craftsman god's name.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vishvakarma-c01",
        "vishvakarma-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia membuat kereta para deva dan senjata, termasuk vajra Indra. Putrinya Sanjna menikah dengan Surya. Dalam Ramayana, Nala adalah putra Vishvakarma yang membantu Rama. Mackenzie menuturkan Vishwakarman membangun kediaman Varuna di dalam laut.",
      "en": "He makes the devas' chariots and weapons including Indra's vajra. His daughter Sanjna is married to Surya. In the Ramayana Nala is Vishvakarma's son who helps Rama. Mackenzie recounts Vishwakarman building Varuna's dwelling within the sea.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "vishvakarma-c03",
        "vishvakarma-c04",
        "vishvakarma-c05",

  "story_mode": null,

  "abilities": [

      "title": "The Project Gutenberg eBook of Indian Myth and Legend, by Donald A. Mackenzie.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "vishvakarma-c01",
      "source_id": "vishvakarma-s1",
      "quote": "is a craftsman deity and the divine architect of the devas in contemporary Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vishvakarma adalah dewa perajin dan arsitek ilahi para deva dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Vishvakarma is a craftsman deity and divine architect of the devas in Hinduism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vishvakarma-c02",
      "source_id": "vishvakarma-s1",
      "quote": "Vishvakarma or Vishvakarman (Sanskrit: विश्वकर्मा, lit. 'all maker', IAST: Viśvakarmā) is a craftsman deity and the divine architect of the devas in contemporary Hinduism. In the early texts, the craftsman deity was known as Tvastar and the word \"Vishvakarma\" was originally used as an epithet for any powerful deity.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama itu semula merupakan epitet dewa kuat, kemudian menjadi nama dewa perajin.",
        "en": "The name was originally an epithet for powerful deities and later became the craftsman god's name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vishvakarma-c03",
      "source_id": "vishvakarma-s1",
      "quote": "Vishvakarma crafted all of the chariots of the devas and weapons including the Vajra of the god Indra. Vishvakarma was related to the sun god Surya through his daughter Sanjna. According to the legend, when Sanjna left her house due to Surya's energy, Vishvakarma reduced the energy and created various other weapons using it.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia membuat kereta para deva dan senjata, termasuk vajra Indra.",
        "en": "He makes the devas' chariots and weapons including Indra's vajra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vishvakarma-c04",
      "source_id": "vishvakarma-s1",
      "quote": "Vishvakarma was related to the sun god Surya through his daughter Sanjna.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Putrinya Sanjna menikah dengan Surya.",
        "en": "His daughter Sanjna is married to Surya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vishvakarma-c05",
      "source_id": "vishvakarma-s1",
      "quote": "According to the epic Ramayana, the vanara (forest-man or monkey) Nala was the son of Vishvakarma, created to aid the avatar Rama.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Ramayana, Nala adalah putra Vishvakarma yang membantu Rama.",
        "en": "In the Ramayana Nala is Vishvakarma's son who helps Rama."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "vishvakarma-c06",
      "source_id": "vishvakarma-s2",
      "quote": "The heaven of Varuna was constructed by Vishwakarman\r\n(Twashtri) within the sea.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 422",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menuturkan Vishwakarman membangun kediaman Varuna di dalam laut.",
        "en": "Mackenzie recounts Vishwakarman building Varuna's dwelling within the sea."
      }
    }
  ],

      "id": "Dyaus Pita adalah dewa langit Rigveda dan pasangan Prithvi, dewi bumi. Keduanya merupakan orang tua arketipal dalam Rigveda.",
      "en": "Dyaus Pita is the Rigvedic sky deity and consort of Prithvi, the earth goddess. Together they are archetypal parents in the Rigveda.",

      ]

    {
      "id": "Kata dyaus tanpa kata ayah menunjuk langit siang. Kitab Weda menyebut Dyaus Pita bersama Prithvi Mata, Ibu Bumi. Ia diumpamakan sebagai kuda hitam bertabur mutiara yang menggambarkan langit malam. Mackenzie menghubungkan Dyaus dengan gambaran banteng merah yang gemuruhnya adalah guntur.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "dyaus-pita-c01",
      "source_id": "dyaus-pita-s1",
      "quote": "Dyaus (Vedic Sanskrit: द्यौस्, IAST: Dyáus) or Dyauspitr (Vedic Sanskrit: द्यौष्पितृ, IAST: Dyáuṣpitṛ́) is the Rigvedic sky deity. His consort is Prithvi, the earth goddess, and together they are the archetypal parents in the Rigveda.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dyaus Pita adalah dewa langit Rigveda dan pasangan Prithvi, dewi bumi.",
        "en": "Dyaus Pita is the Rigvedic sky deity and consort of Prithvi, the earth goddess."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dyaus-pita-c02",
      "source_id": "dyaus-pita-s1",
      "quote": "Dyaus (Vedic Sanskrit: द्यौस्, IAST: Dyáus) or Dyauspitr (Vedic Sanskrit: द्यौष्पितृ, IAST: Dyáuṣpitṛ́) is the Rigvedic sky deity. His consort is Prithvi, the earth goddess, and together they are the archetypal parents in the Rigveda.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keduanya merupakan orang tua arketipal dalam Rigveda.",
        "en": "Together they are archetypal parents in the Rigveda."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dyaus-pita-c03",
      "source_id": "dyaus-pita-s1",
      "quote": "The noun dyaús (when used without the pitṛ́ 'father') refers to the daylight sky, and occurs frequently in the Rigveda, as an entity. The sky in Vedic writing was described as rising in three tiers: avamá, madhyamá, and uttamá or tṛtī́ya.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata dyaus tanpa kata ayah menunjuk langit siang.",
        "en": "The word dyaus without the word father refers to the daylight sky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dyaus-pita-c04",
      "source_id": "dyaus-pita-s1",
      "quote": "Dyáuṣ Pitṛ́ appears in hymns with Prithvi Mata, 'Mother Earth' in the ancient Vedic scriptures of Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kitab Weda menyebut Dyaus Pita bersama Prithvi Mata, Ibu Bumi.",
        "en": "Vedic scriptures mention Dyaus Pita with Prithvi Mata, Mother Earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dyaus-pita-c05",
      "source_id": "dyaus-pita-s1",
      "quote": "Dyauṣ is also stated to be like a black stallion studded with pearls in a simile with the night sky.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia diumpamakan sebagai kuda hitam bertabur mutiara yang menggambarkan langit malam.",
        "en": "He is likened to a black stallion studded with pearls representing the night sky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dyaus-pita-c06",
      "source_id": "dyaus-pita-s2",
      "quote": "Dyaus is sometimes\r\nreferred to as a ruddy bull, whose bellowing is the\r\nthunder;",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 159",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menghubungkan Dyaus dengan gambaran banteng merah yang gemuruhnya adalah guntur.",
        "en": "Mackenzie connects Dyaus with the image of a ruddy bull whose bellowing is thunder."
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Gandharvas are a class of celestial beings in Indian religions associated with music and dance.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gandharva adalah golongan makhluk surgawi dalam agama India yang berhubungan dengan musik dan tari. Dalam Hindu mereka merupakan dewa kecil yang menjadi musisi ilahi.",
      "en": "Gandharvas are a class of celestial beings in Indian religions associated with music and dance. In Hinduism they are minor deities serving as divine musicians.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gandharva-c01",
        "gandharva-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam sumber Weda, gandharva juga dapat menunjuk satu makhluk surgawi yang menjaga Soma. Gandharva umumnya disebut sebagai suami para apsara. Mereka biasanya hidup di Svarga dan melayani istana Indra. Mackenzie menggambarkan musik Gandharva mengiringi tarian apsara.",
      "en": "In Vedic sources gandharva can also designate a single celestial being guarding Soma. Gandharvas are generally called the husbands of apsaras. They usually live in Svarga and serve Indra's court. Mackenzie describes Gandharva music accompanying apsara dances.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gandharva-c03",
        "gandharva-c04",
        "gandharva-c05",
        "gandharva-c06"
      ]
    }

      "id": "gandharva-c01",
      "source_id": "gandharva-s1",
      "quote": "A gandharva (Sanskrit: गन्धर्व) is a member of a class of celestial beings in Indian religions, such as Hinduism, Buddhism, and Jainism, whose males are divine performers such as musicians and singers, and the females are divine dancers. In Hinduism, they are regarded to be the celestial demigods who serve as the musicians of the devas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gandharva adalah golongan makhluk surgawi dalam agama India yang berhubungan dengan musik dan tari.",
        "en": "Gandharvas are a class of celestial beings in Indian religions associated with music and dance."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gandharva-c02",
      "source_id": "gandharva-s1",
      "quote": "In Hinduism, the gandharvas (Sanskrit: गन्धर्व, romanized: gandharva) are a class of minor deities who serve as divine musicians in Hindu mythology.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Hindu mereka merupakan dewa kecil yang menjadi musisi ilahi.",
        "en": "In Hinduism they are minor deities serving as divine musicians."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gandharva-c03",
      "source_id": "gandharva-s1",
      "quote": "gandharva is presented as a celestial being (dwelling near the Sun / in the heavenly waters) which watches over the Soma",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam sumber Weda, gandharva juga dapat menunjuk satu makhluk surgawi yang menjaga Soma.",
        "en": "In Vedic sources gandharva can also designate a single celestial being guarding Soma."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gandharva-c04",
      "source_id": "gandharva-s1",
      "quote": "They are generally the husbands of apsaras. They are described as handsome beings who wear fragrant attire and are passionate about women.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gandharva umumnya disebut sebagai suami para apsara.",
        "en": "Gandharvas are generally called the husbands of apsaras."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gandharva-c05",
      "source_id": "gandharva-s1",
      "quote": "Gandharvas usually live in Svarga and serve at Indra's court, though they also have their own realm, called the Gandharvaloka.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka biasanya hidup di Svarga dan melayani istana Indra.",
        "en": "They usually live in Svarga and serve Indra's court."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gandharva-c06",
      "source_id": "gandharva-s2",
      "quote": "When they play on their divine instruments the fairy-like\r\nApsaras, who are all females, dance merrily.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 471",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie menggambarkan musik Gandharva mengiringi tarian apsara.",
        "en": "Mackenzie describes Gandharva music accompanying apsara dances."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shukra merupakan resi mitologis dan dewa yang memimpin planet Venus dalam Hindu. Nama lainnya Ushanas dan Kavya.",
      "en": "Shukra is a mythological sage and presiding god of Venus in Hinduism. His other names are Ushanas and Kavya.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shukra-c01",
        "shukra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Rigveda ia merupakan resi bijak bernama Kavya Ushanas dan belum dikaitkan dengan Venus. Teks Sanskerta kemudian menyamakan Ushanas, Kavya, Shukra, dan Bhargava sebagai nama tokoh yang sama. Dalam teks kemudian ia disebut putra atau cucu Bhrigu dan putra Kavyamata. Purana belakangan menetapkan perannya sebagai pendeta dan guru para asura.",
      "en": "In the Rigveda he is the wise seer Kavya Ushanas and is not yet associated with Venus. Later Sanskrit texts identify Ushanas, Kavya, Shukra, and Bhargava as names of the same figure. In later texts he is called Bhrigu's son or grandson and Kavyamata's son. Later Puranas establish his role as priest and preceptor of the asuras.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "shukra-c03",
        "shukra-c04",
        "shukra-c05",
        "shukra-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",

      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "shukra-c02",
      "source_id": "shukra-s1",
      "quote": "also known as Ushanas and Kavya, is a mythological sage as well as the presiding god of the planet Venus in Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama lainnya Ushanas dan Kavya.",
        "en": "His other names are Ushanas and Kavya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shukra-c03",
      "source_id": "shukra-s1",
      "quote": "The figure later known as Shukra appears in the Vedic corpus primarily under the name Kavya Ushanas. ... Neither is there any attestation made to suggest that belonged to the Bhrigu clan nor his association with the planet Venus.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Rigveda ia merupakan resi bijak bernama Kavya Ushanas dan belum dikaitkan dengan Venus.",
        "en": "In the Rigveda he is the wise seer Kavya Ushanas and is not yet associated with Venus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shukra-c04",
      "source_id": "shukra-s1",
      "quote": "While the Vedic texts primarily refer to the figure as Ushanas Kavya, later Sanskrit literature introduces Shukra and Bhargava (lit. 'descendant of Bhrigu') serving as common epithets for the  sage named Ushanas. As a result, the names Ushanas, Kavya, Shukra and Bhargava came to be treated as referring to the same individual in the later tradition.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Teks Sanskerta kemudian menyamakan Ushanas, Kavya, Shukra, dan Bhargava sebagai nama tokoh yang sama.",
        "en": "Later Sanskrit texts identify Ushanas, Kavya, Shukra, and Bhargava as names of the same figure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shukra-c05",
      "source_id": "shukra-s1",
      "quote": "Ushanas is mentioned as the son (or grandson in some texts) of the sage Bhrigu, one of the seven revered sages originating from the creator god Brahma. His mother is called Kavyamata (lit. 'mother of Kavya').",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam teks kemudian ia disebut putra atau cucu Bhrigu dan putra Kavyamata.",
        "en": "In later texts he is called Bhrigu's son or grandson and Kavyamata's son."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "shukra-c06",
      "source_id": "shukra-s1",
      "quote": "By the time of the later Puranas, however, his role as the priest and preceptor of the asuras is firmly concretised.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 6",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Purana belakangan menetapkan perannya sebagai pendeta dan guru para asura.",
        "en": "Later Puranas establish his role as priest and preceptor of the asuras."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Dalam tradisi Nepal, Kumari adalah perwujudan hidup Taleju Bhawani yang dipuja melalui seorang anak perempuan Newar.",
    "en": "In Nepalese tradition Kumari is a living embodiment of Taleju Bhawani venerated through a Newar girl.",
    "claim_ids": [

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi Nepal, Kumari adalah perwujudan hidup Taleju Bhawani yang dipuja melalui seorang anak perempuan Newar. Praktik ini melibatkan pemuja Hindu dan Buddha di Lembah Kathmandu.",
      "en": "In Nepalese tradition Kumari is a living embodiment of Taleju Bhawani venerated through a Newar girl. The practice involves Hindu and Buddhist devotees in the Kathmandu Valley.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kumari-c01",
        "kumari-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kumari berasal dari Sanskerta dalam arti perawan atau gadis muda belum menikah. Legenda pendirian menuturkan Taleju menghilang setelah pertemuan rahasianya dengan raja diketahui. Dalam mimpi, Taleju meminta raja mencari gadis Shakya tempat ia akan mewujud. Dewan Pariwisata Nepal menyatakan calon Kumari dipilih dari keluarga Buddhis klan Shakya.",
      "en": "Kumari derives from Sanskrit meaning virgin or young unmarried girl. The foundation legend recounts Taleju disappearing after her secret meetings with the king are discovered. In a dream Taleju asks the king to find a Shakya girl in whom she will manifest. The Nepal Tourism Board states that a Kumari candidate is selected from a Buddhist family of the Shakya clan.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "kumari-c03",
        "kumari-c04",
        "kumari-c05",
        "kumari-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "kumari-c01",
      "source_id": "kumari-s1",
      "quote": "also known as the Living Goddess, is a centuries‑old tradition in Nepal in which a prepubescent girl from the Newar community is venerated as a living embodiment of the goddess Taleju Bhawani (a form of Durga).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Nepal, Kumari adalah perwujudan hidup Taleju Bhawani yang dipuja melalui seorang anak perempuan Newar.",
        "en": "In Nepalese tradition Kumari is a living embodiment of Taleju Bhawani venerated through a Newar girl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumari-c02",
      "source_id": "kumari-s1",
      "quote": "The practice is central to the religious life of the Kathmandu Valley and draws devotees from both Hindu and Buddhist traditions.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Praktik ini melibatkan pemuja Hindu dan Buddha di Lembah Kathmandu.",
        "en": "The practice involves Hindu and Buddhist devotees in the Kathmandu Valley."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumari-c03",
      "source_id": "kumari-s1",
      "quote": "The word Kumari is derived from Sanskrit, where it means \"virgin\" or \"young unmarried girl\". In the Newar language the term is also rendered as kumhaḥ (𑐎𑐸𑐩𑑂𑐴𑑅).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kumari berasal dari Sanskerta dalam arti perawan atau gadis muda belum menikah.",
        "en": "Kumari derives from Sanskrit meaning virgin or young unmarried girl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumari-c04",
      "source_id": "kumari-s1",
      "quote": "the goddess Taleju would visit the king nightly to play Tripasa (a dice game), on condition that he tell no one of their meetings. One night the king’s daughter followed him and discovered the goddess, who was angered and vanished.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda pendirian menuturkan Taleju menghilang setelah pertemuan rahasianya dengan raja diketahui.",
        "en": "The foundation legend recounts Taleju disappearing after her secret meetings with the king are discovered."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumari-c05",
      "source_id": "kumari-s1",
      "quote": "Taleju later appeared in a dream, telling the king to search for a young Shakya girl in whom she would manifest.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mimpi, Taleju meminta raja mencari gadis Shakya tempat ia akan mewujud.",
        "en": "In a dream Taleju asks the king to find a Shakya girl in whom she will manifest."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "kumari-c06",
      "source_id": "kumari-s2",
      "quote": "A Kumari candidate is selected from a Buddhist family of Shakya clan.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dewan Pariwisata Nepal menyatakan calon Kumari dipilih dari keluarga Buddhis klan Shakya.",
        "en": "The Nepal Tourism Board states that a Kumari candidate is selected from a Buddhist family of the Shakya clan."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Shesha adalah naga ilahi, raja ular, dan makhluk primordial penciptaan dalam Hindu.",
    "en": "Shesha is a divine naga, king of serpents, and primordial creation being in Hinduism.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "sesa-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Shesha adalah naga ilahi, raja ular, dan makhluk primordial penciptaan dalam Hindu. Ia juga disebut Ananta, dalam arti tanpa akhir.",
      "en": "Shesha is a divine naga, king of serpents, and primordial creation being in Hinduism. He is also called Ananta, meaning without end.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sesa-c01",
        "sesa-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Vishnu dalam bentuk Narayana lazim digambarkan beristirahat di atas Shesha bersama Lakshmi. Dalam tradisi, ia berinkarnasi sebagai Lakshmana dan Balarama. Ikonografinya dapat berkepala lima, tujuh, atau sepuluh, sementara teks klasik sering menyebut seribu kepala. Mackenzie juga menyebut Balarama sebagai inkarnasi ular dunia Shesha.",
      "en": "Vishnu as Narayana is commonly depicted resting on Shesha with Lakshmi. In tradition he incarnates as Lakshmana and Balarama. His iconography can have five, seven, or ten hoods, while classical texts often describe a thousand heads. Mackenzie also calls Balarama an incarnation of the world serpent Shesha.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "sesa-c03",
        "sesa-c04",
        "sesa-c05",
        "sesa-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,
  "did_you_know": null,

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "sesa-c01",
      "source_id": "sesa-s1",
      "quote": "is a serpentine naga (serpent deity), and king of the serpents (Nagaraja), and a primordial being of creation in Hinduism.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Shesha adalah naga ilahi, raja ular, dan makhluk primordial penciptaan dalam Hindu.",
        "en": "Shesha is a divine naga, king of serpents, and primordial creation being in Hinduism."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sesa-c02",
      "source_id": "sesa-s1",
      "quote": "He is also known as Ananta ... or Ananta Shesha",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga disebut Ananta, dalam arti tanpa akhir.",
        "en": "He is also called Ananta, meaning without end."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sesa-c03",
      "source_id": "sesa-s1",
      "quote": "Vishnu, in his Narayana form, is traditionally depicted as resting on Shesha alongside his consort Lakshmi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vishnu dalam bentuk Narayana lazim digambarkan beristirahat di atas Shesha bersama Lakshmi.",
        "en": "Vishnu as Narayana is commonly depicted resting on Shesha with Lakshmi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sesa-c04",
      "source_id": "sesa-s1",
      "quote": "Shesha is traditionally believed to have incarnated on Earth as Lakshmana, the younger brother of Vishnu's incarnation Rama during the Treta Yuga, and as Balarama, the elder brother of Vishnu's incarnation Krishna during the Dvapara Yuga.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi, ia berinkarnasi sebagai Lakshmana dan Balarama.",
        "en": "In tradition he incarnates as Lakshmana and Balarama."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sesa-c05",
      "source_id": "sesa-s1",
      "quote": "Shesha is generally depicted with a large coiled form floating through cosmic space or upon the Ocean of Milk, forming the bed upon which Vishnu rests. In iconography, he is depicted with multiple hoods—often five, seven or ten—though classical texts frequently describe him with one thousand crowned heads.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ikonografinya dapat berkepala lima, tujuh, atau sepuluh, sementara teks klasik sering menyebut seribu kepala.",
        "en": "His iconography can have five, seven, or ten hoods, while classical texts often describe a thousand heads."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "sesa-c06",
      "source_id": "sesa-s2",
      "quote": "Balarama is an incarnation of the world serpent\r\nShesha.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 741",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie juga menyebut Balarama sebagai inkarnasi ular dunia Shesha.",
        "en": "Mackenzie also calls Balarama an incarnation of the world serpent Shesha."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Aditya adalah kelompok dewa Hindu yang umumnya dianggap dewa matahari dan keturunan Aditi.",
    "en": "Adityas are a class of Hindu deities generally regarded as solar gods and Aditi's offspring.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "adityas-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Aditya adalah kelompok dewa Hindu yang umumnya dianggap dewa matahari dan keturunan Aditi. Jumlah mereka enam sampai delapan dalam Rigveda dan meningkat menjadi dua belas dalam Brahmana.",
      "en": "Adityas are a class of Hindu deities generally regarded as solar gods and Aditi's offspring. Their number is six to eight in the Rigveda and increases to twelve in the Brahmanas.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adityas-c01",
        "adityas-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Mahabharata dan Purana, Kashyapa disebut sebagai ayah mereka. Rigveda menggambarkan Aditya terang, murni, dan bebas dari kepalsuan. Dua belas Aditya dipandang mewakili dua belas bulan dan aspek Matahari. Mackenzie membahas Mitra, Varuna, dan Aryaman sebagai anggota kelompok dewa Aditya.",
      "en": "In the Mahabharata and Puranas Kashyapa is called their father. The Rigveda describes the Adityas as bright, pure, and free from falsehood. The twelve Adityas are regarded as representing twelve months and aspects of the Sun. Mackenzie discusses Mitra, Varuna, and Aryaman as members of the Aditya group.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "adityas-c03",
        "adityas-c04",
        "adityas-c05",
        "adityas-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

  "modern_depictions": [],

      "id": "adityas-s1",

    {
      "id": "adityas-s2",

      "title": "The Project Gutenberg eBook of Indian Myth and Legend, by Donald A. Mackenzie.",

      "published": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "adityas-c01",
      "source_id": "adityas-s1",
      "quote": "refers to a class of Hindu deities. They are usually presented as solar deities, and the offspring of the Goddess Aditi.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Aditya adalah kelompok dewa Hindu yang umumnya dianggap dewa matahari dan keturunan Aditi.",
        "en": "Adityas are a class of Hindu deities generally regarded as solar gods and Aditi's offspring."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adityas-c02",
      "source_id": "adityas-s1",
      "quote": "They are first attested in the Rig Veda, where they are 6–8 in number, all male. The number increases to 12 in the Brahmanas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jumlah mereka enam sampai delapan dalam Rigveda dan meningkat menjadi dua belas dalam Brahmana.",
        "en": "Their number is six to eight in the Rigveda and increases to twelve in the Brahmanas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adityas-c03",
      "source_id": "adityas-s1",
      "quote": "The  epic Mahabharata and the Puranic scriptures mention the sage Kashyapa  as their father,",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Mahabharata dan Purana, Kashyapa disebut sebagai ayah mereka.",
        "en": "In the Mahabharata and Puranas Kashyapa is called their father."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adityas-c04",
      "source_id": "adityas-s1",
      "quote": "The Aditya have been described in the Rig Veda as bright and pure as streams of water, free from all guile and falsehood, blameless, perfect.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rigveda menggambarkan Aditya terang, murni, dan bebas dari kepalsuan.",
        "en": "The Rigveda describes the Adityas as bright, pure, and free from falsehood."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adityas-c05",
      "source_id": "adityas-s1",
      "quote": "In present-day usage in Sanskrit, the term Aditya has been made singular in contrast to Vedic Adityas, and is being used synonymously with Surya, the Sun. The twelve Adityas are believed to represent the twelve months in the calendar and the twelve aspects of Sun. Since they are twelve in number, they are referred as DvadashAdityas.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 4",
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dua belas Aditya dipandang mewakili dua belas bulan dan aspek Matahari.",
        "en": "The twelve Adityas are regarded as representing twelve months and aspects of the Sun."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adityas-c06",
      "source_id": "adityas-s2",
      "quote": "Mitra as Mithra with Varuna, and a third vague god,\r\nAryaman, belong to an early group of equal deities called\r\nthe Adityas, or “Celestial deities”.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 241",
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mackenzie membahas Mitra, Varuna, dan Aryaman sebagai anggota kelompok dewa Aditya.",
        "en": "Mackenzie discusses Mitra, Varuna, and Aryaman as members of the Aditya group."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

    "en": "Makara is a legendary sea creature in Hindu mythology.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "makara-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Makara adalah makhluk laut legendaris dalam mitologi Hindu. Ia menjadi tunggangan Ganga, Narmada, dan Varuna.",
      "en": "Makara is a legendary sea creature in Hindu mythology. It serves as the mount of Ganga, Narmada, and Varuna.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "makara-c01",
        "makara-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Makara dianggap penjaga gerbang dan ambang kuil serta ruang takhta. Kata Sanskerta makara berarti hewan laut atau buaya. Bentuk umumnya menggabungkan bagian depan hewan darat dengan bagian belakang hewan air. Dalam karya Sinhala, makara menggabungkan bagian beberapa hewan, seperti belalai gajah dan rahang buaya.",
      "en": "Makaras are regarded as guardians of gateways and thresholds of temples and throne rooms. The Sanskrit word makara means sea animal or crocodile. Its common form combines a terrestrial animal's front with an aquatic animal's rear. In Sinhalese artwork a makara combines parts of several animals such as elephant trunk and crocodile jaws.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "makara-c03",
        "makara-c04",
        "makara-c05",
        "makara-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "cultural_context": null,
  "etymology": null,
  "story_mode": null,

  "modern_depictions": [],
  "tradition_vs_modern": null,

  "sources": [
    {
      "id": "makara-s1",
      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makara",
      "title": "Makara",
      "author": null,

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "makara-c01",
      "source_id": "makara-s1",
      "quote": "Makara (Sanskrit: मकर, romanized: Makara) is a legendary sea-creature in Hindu mythology. In Hindu astrology, Makara is equivalent to the  Zodiac sign Capricorn.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 1",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makara adalah makhluk laut legendaris dalam mitologi Hindu.",
        "en": "Makara is a legendary sea creature in Hindu mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "makara-c02",
      "source_id": "makara-s1",
      "quote": "Makara appears as the vahana (vehicle) of the river goddess Ganga, Narmada, and of the god of the ocean, Varuna.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia menjadi tunggangan Ganga, Narmada, dan Varuna.",
        "en": "It serves as the mount of Ganga, Narmada, and Varuna."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "makara-c03",
      "source_id": "makara-s1",
      "quote": "Makara are considered guardians of gateways and thresholds, protecting throne rooms as well as entryways to temples;",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 2",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makara dianggap penjaga gerbang dan ambang kuil serta ruang takhta.",
        "en": "Makaras are regarded as guardians of gateways and thresholds of temples and throne rooms."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "makara-c04",
      "source_id": "makara-s1",
      "quote": "Makara is a Sanskrit word which means \"sea-animal, crocodile\".",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 3",
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Sanskerta makara berarti hewan laut atau buaya.",
        "en": "The Sanskrit word makara means sea animal or crocodile."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "makara-c05",
      "source_id": "makara-s1",
      "quote": "It is generally depicted as half terrestrial animal in the frontal part (stag, deer, or elephant) and half aquatic animal in the hind part (usually of a fish, a dolphin, or a snake, though sometimes a peacock or even a floral tail is depicted).",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 5",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk umumnya menggabungkan bagian depan hewan darat dengan bagian belakang hewan air.",
        "en": "Its common form combines a terrestrial animal's front with an aquatic animal's rear."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "makara-c06",
      "source_id": "makara-s1",
      "quote": "In Sinhalese ancient artwork Makara is made up of body parts of six or seven animals such as the trunk of the elephant, jaws of the crocodile, ears of the mouse or ape, extruding teeth of wild swine, the tail plume of the peacock and feet of the lion.",
      "locator": "Article text, paragraph 7",
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam karya Sinhala, makara menggabungkan bagian beberapa hewan, seperti belalai gajah dan rahang buaya.",
        "en": "In Sinhalese artwork a makara combines parts of several animals such as elephant trunk and crocodile jaws."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Katalog LACMA yang berjudul Devi on a Mythical Aquatic Creature (Makara) ditemukan. Teks dan unduhannya belum terverifikasi sehingga klaim museum belum dipakai. Sumber pendamping diperlukan."
